    Light-Cone Friedel Oscillations: a Window into van Hove Superconductivity

    Authors: Archisman Panigrahi, Vladislav Poliakov, Leonid Levitov

    Abstract: …to measure whether---and how strongly---that saddle-point quasiparticle sector is gapped. A superconducting gap near vH points suppresses the oscillations in the directions corresponding to peaks in the gap function angular dependence, a behavior that provides a signature of vH-related pairing at STM-accessible length scales. This STM protocol, targeting vH… ▽ More Van Hove (vH) singularities can enhance superconductivity by generating a large pairing susceptibility. Yet, it remains challenging to experimentally establish whether pairing really occurs near these points. We show that the saddle-point dispersion near a vH point gives rise to a `light-cone' vH-selective behavior of Friedel oscillations, which is drastically different from the familiar concentric circles. Each vH point maps onto its own real-space light-cone sector, allowing STM around a single impurity to measure whether---and how strongly---that saddle-point quasiparticle sector is gapped. A superconducting gap near vH points suppresses the oscillations in the directions corresponding to peaks in the gap function angular dependence, a behavior that provides a signature of vH-related pairing at STM-accessible length scales. This STM protocol, targeting vH superconductivity, is capable of determining whether superconducting pairing emerges near the vH points or elsewhere on the Fermi surface. △ Less

    quant-ph cond-mat.stat-mech

    Locality in Dissipative Ground-State Preparation

    Authors: Qicheng Tang, Ruhanshi Barad, Xueda Wen

    Abstract: …free-fermion Hamiltonians, using a quadratic Lindbladian with linear jump operators for the dissipative ground-state preparation. For translation-invariant targets, Fourier correspondence relates spatial locality to momentum-space analyticity, allowing us to derive a complete spectral criterion for the existence of exponentially local linear jump operators t… ▽ More Can the ground state of a local Hamiltonian be prepared by local dissipation? We address this question for free-fermion Hamiltonians, using a quadratic Lindbladian with linear jump operators for the dissipative ground-state preparation. For translation-invariant targets, Fourier correspondence relates spatial locality to momentum-space analyticity, allowing us to derive a complete spectral criterion for the existence of exponentially local linear jump operators that prepare the ground state. This criterion leads to a no-go theorem that, sign-changing Fermi-level crossings exclude exponentially local linear jump operators for preparing the ground state. While these crossings are the complete obstruction in one dimension, additional obstructions generally arise in higher dimensions. We illustrate these additional obstructions using two quadratic-band-touching models with algebraically decaying ground-state correlations. We further show that short-range ground-state correlations, although not necessary, are sufficient for local dissipative preparation even without translation invariance. Our results establish how the spectral structure of the target Hamiltonian governs the locality of dissipative ground-state preparation. △ Less

    Some remarks on robust features of surfaces in 4-space

    Authors: Jorge Luiz Deolindo-Silva

    Abstract: …with lines and hyperplanes. We investigate the local and multi-local singularities of orthogonal projections and height functions that describe these contacts and their corresponding geometric loci on the surface. At an $A_4$-singularity of a height function, we show that the $A_3$-curve and the $A_1A_2$-curve are generically tangent with contact of order… ▽ More We study robust features of smooth surfaces in 4-space associated with contact with lines and hyperplanes. We investigate the local and multi-local singularities of orthogonal projections and height functions that describe these contacts and their corresponding geometric loci on the surface. At an $A_4$-singularity of a height function, we show that the $A_3$-curve and the $A_1A_2$-curve are generically tangent with contact of order $2$. We further establish criteria for the versality of the families of height functions and orthogonal projections in terms of the regularity of the $B_2$-curve and $S_2$-curve, respectively. Finally, we obtain the $[A_2]$- and $(A_0S_0)_2$-curves arising from multi-local singularities of orthogonal projections and describe their tangency properties at the $B_3$- and $H_3$-singularities. △ Less

    Abstract: …, every array in the corresponding class admits a unique Euler evolution, even among bounded energy-admissible weak velocity solutions. If $I_Θ<\infty$, there exists a compactly supported array in the same class admitting two distinct unforced Lagrangian Euler evolutions from the same initial datum. The criterion holds for arbitrary nondecreasing envelope… ▽ More We establish a sharp uniqueness--nonuniqueness criterion for the two-dimensional incompressible Euler equations within a class of stationary arrays of disjoint neutral vortices. Let $Θ$ be any finite nondecreasing envelope for the vorticity $L^p$ norms, and set $I_Θ=\int_{p_0}^\infty Θ(p)^{-1}\,dp$. If $I_Θ=\infty$, every array in the corresponding class admits a unique Euler evolution, even among bounded energy-admissible weak velocity solutions. If $I_Θ<\infty$, there exists a compactly supported array in the same class admitting two distinct unforced Lagrangian Euler evolutions from the same initial datum. The criterion holds for arbitrary nondecreasing envelopes $Θ$. △ Less

    The IRAC Distant Cluster Survey

    Authors: Karolina Garcia, Anthony H. Gonzalez, Mark Brodwin, Peter Eisenhardt, Tyler George, Nadia Hansen, S. A. Stanford, Daniel Stern, Khunanon Thongkham, Ariane Trudeau, Michael J. I. Brown, Buell T. Jannuzi

    Abstract: …, corresponding to a median halo mass of $1.1 \times 10^{14} M_\odot$. A positional comparison with 39 independently confirmed literature clusters yields a median centroid offset of 211 kpc. The IDCS catalog provides a cluster sample spanning a wide redshift range, with a selection function characterized through simulations and is a valuable resource for stu… ▽ More We present the Infrared Array Camera (IRAC) Distant Cluster Survey (IDCS), a catalog of 825 galaxy cluster and group candidates identified in the Boötes field using the PZWav detection code. Candidates are selected using photometric redshift probability distributions derived from twelve-band optical and infrared imaging. The catalog spans $0.08 < z < 2.0$ (median $z = 0.93$) and includes 147 systems at $z \geq 1.5$. For $z < 1.5$, simulations indicate a purity of $93\%$ at the adopted S/N $\geq 4$ threshold and $50\%$ completeness at $\log(M_{200c}/M_\odot) = 13.74 \pm 0.01$. We validate the cluster photometric redshifts through comparison with spectroscopic data, finding $σ_z/(1+z)=0.017$. Including 18 previously published IRAC Shallow Cluster Survey (ISCS) systems, a total of 80 IDCS cluster candidates are now confirmed through spectroscopy and/or X-ray observations. DESI DR1 spectroscopy provides redshifts for 38 systems, including 23 newly confirmed clusters. These DESI-confirmed systems span $0.12 < z < 0.93$ and have at least 10 spectroscopic members each, from which we measure a median velocity dispersion of $\sim 490$ km s$^{-1}$, corresponding to a median halo mass of $1.1 \times 10^{14} M_\odot$. A positional comparison with 39 independently confirmed literature clusters yields a median centroid offset of 211 kpc. The IDCS catalog provides a cluster sample spanning a wide redshift range, with a selection function characterized through simulations and is a valuable resource for studies of large-scale structure, galaxy evolution in dense environments, and the calibration of cluster detection techniques for current and upcoming wide-field surveys, including LSST, Roman, and Euclid. △ Less

    cs.CV

    PhysCount: Do MLLMs Count Visual Appearances or Physical Entities?

    Authors: Lidong Lu, Jiaxin Lv, Liangrui Dong, Xinyi Mao, Lidong Zou, Zhenquan Li, Tong Lu, Guo Chen

    Abstract: …Four complementary question families test the enumeration of appearances, the enumeration of physical entities, image-formation-specific counting, and appearance--entity correspondence. We measure both accuracy on each family and the proportion of groups answered entirely correctly. Across 40 MLLMs, counting visible appearances is substantially easier than… ▽ More Images reveal appearances, but counting often asks about physical objects. A single entity may be visible directly, in several reflections, or through glass, so detecting every visible occurrence does not determine how many entities are present. Existing visual counting benchmarks rarely separate these two operations. We introduce PhysCount, a benchmark for evaluating whether multimodal large language models (MLLMs) can distinguish what appears in an image from what physically exists in the scene. PhysCount contains 546 real-world images and 4,595 human-written questions organized into 651 target-specific groups. For each group, annotators locate the relevant appearances, label how each is observed, and identify appearances of the same entity. Four complementary question families test the enumeration of appearances, the enumeration of physical entities, image-formation-specific counting, and appearance--entity correspondence. We measure both accuracy on each family and the proportion of groups answered entirely correctly. Across 40 MLLMs, counting visible appearances is substantially easier than recovering the identities that connect them. Even the strongest model answers every question correctly in only 13.36\% of groups. Performance declines when one entity produces many appearances or when its appearances cross observation modes. Higher image resolution improves access to visual evidence but brings limited gains in complete-group accuracy, whereas answering related questions jointly produces a marked improvement. These results expose a gap between recognizing what is visible and correctly recovering the physical entities behind those observations. △ Less

    Superselection Rigidity of Topological Calderbank--Shor--Steane Codes: From Koszul-Complex Models to Nonsplit Extensions

    Authors: Hao Song

    Abstract: …retains cross-degree gluing. Pointlike, looplike, and higher-dimensional excitations correspond to degrees $\ell=1,2,\ldots$, respectively. Our matrix-level Schanuel-lemma method proves single-degree superselection rigidity: if $\mathscr E_σ$ is supported in a single degree $\ell$, then $E_σ^\ell$ and $\ell$ fix the stable translation-invariant Clifford cla… ▽ More Can excitation structure classify topological quantum codes up to finite-depth local circuits? For translation-invariant topological Calderbank--Shor--Steane (CSS) codes in arbitrary dimensions, we introduce excitation superselection profiles $\mathscr E_σ$ ($σ=X,Z$). Their cohomology layers $E_σ^\ell:=H^\ell(\mathscr E_σ)$ encode excitation types, fusion rules, and translation action, while $\mathscr E_σ$ retains cross-degree gluing. Pointlike, looplike, and higher-dimensional excitations correspond to degrees $\ell=1,2,\ldots$, respectively. Our matrix-level Schanuel-lemma method proves single-degree superselection rigidity: if $\mathscr E_σ$ is supported in a single degree $\ell$, then $E_σ^\ell$ and $\ell$ fix the stable translation-invariant Clifford class under a finite-resolution hypothesis automatic for prime qudits. If $E_σ^\ell$ is also finite, prime-qudit codes decouple into toric-code stacks by Clifford circuits after coarse graining, with the exact minimal threshold set by the translation kernel. We develop a unified topological theory of Koszul-complex models (encompassing toric codes, bivariate-bicycle codes, and Haah's cubic code): for regular models, we compute $E_σ^\ell$, prove $\mathscr E_σ$ is single-degree, express finite-torus logical modules as $\mathrm{Tor}$ groups, and obtain coarse-grained toric-stack normal forms at full length. Two constructions extend this framework to realize any admissible single-degree profile and build multi-degree extensions. The latter yields eight 3D qubit toric-code extensions with identical layers in both Pauli sectors and trivial translation action, yet distinct size-dependent ground-state degeneracies. The seven nonsplit models require blocking to decouple. Thus, cross-degree gluing carries translation-symmetry-enriched topological information invisible to individual layers. △ Less

    CrossWeave: Cross-Modal Scene and Instance Retrieval from Sparse 2D-3D Alignment

    Authors: Aadith Warrier, Gnana Prakash Punnavajhala, Siddharth Tourani, Muhammad Haris Khan, Avinash Sharma, Madhava Krishna

    Abstract: Cross-modal scene and instance retrieval aims to retrieve a corresponding 3D environment or object given query 2D images, serving as a foundational mechanism for spatial reasoning in robotics and AR/VR. Existing methods achieve this by learning alignment only at the level of whole scenes or objects, which provides no supervision for the fine-grained local st… ▽ More Cross-modal scene and instance retrieval aims to retrieve a corresponding 3D environment or object given query 2D images, serving as a foundational mechanism for spatial reasoning in robotics and AR/VR. Existing methods achieve this by learning alignment only at the level of whole scenes or objects, which provides no supervision for the fine-grained local structure within them. In this work, we show that supervision on a set of sparse 2D-3D correspondences, without scene- or instance-level contrastive objectives, is sufficient to train a single model for both scene- and instance-level retrieval. Specifically, we propose $\textbf{CrossWeave}$, a novel framework for cross-modal retrieval that learns sparse patch-point alignment, which alone outperforms existing methods. An optimal transport-based aggregator over the aligned features gives further gains by clustering structurally redundant regions, freeing descriptor capacity for semantically distinct local features. CrossWeave achieves state-of-the-art performance on both scene- and instance-level retrieval on ScanNet and 3RScan with a $\textit{single model}$, whereas existing methods require separate models for each task. Our results suggest that whole-scene encoding is unnecessary for cross-modal retrieval and that sparse local correspondence supervision is a more effective and flexible alternative. △ Less

    Abstract: …reproduces and vastly generalizes all known results for on-shell actions in these classes, with no need for a consistent truncation. Where known, we match the corresponding field theory partition functions at leading order, and our results extend easily to other solutions of type II supergravity. ▽ More We construct equivariantly closed polyforms for the fluxes and the action of (massive) type IIA and type IIB supergravity directly in ten dimensions. We illustrate applications of our formalism to two distinct classes of solutions in massive type IIA.     Zero-freeness for Random Regular Graphs up to the Replica Symmetry Breaking Threshold

    Authors: Zejia Chen, Chunyang Wang, Xinyuan Zhang

    Abstract: …-colorings. Leveraging the standard algorithmic implications of zero-freeness, we obtain deterministic polynomial-time algorithms that multiplicatively approximate the corresponding partition functions at fixed rational parameters throughout this regime, with high probability over the graph. Our algorithmic guarantees significantly improve upon prior work… ▽ More We study antiferromagnetic spin systems on random regular graphs through the lens of the phase transitions characterized by the complex zeros of the partition function. Our motivating examples include the hardcore model and the antiferromagnetic Potts model (including proper $q$-colorings at $β=0$). For both models on random $d$-regular graphs, we establish, with high probability over the graph, zero-free regions throughout the interior of the replica-symmetric regime. Asymptotically, this regime extends up to tree occupation density $$α< α_{\mathrm{RSB}}(d) = (2\log d - 2\log\log d + 2 - 2\log 2 + o_d(1))/d$$ for the hardcore model and up to degree $$d < d_{\mathrm{RSB}}(q) = (2q-1)\log q - 2\log 2 + o_q(1)$$ for proper $q$-colorings. Leveraging the standard algorithmic implications of zero-freeness, we obtain deterministic polynomial-time algorithms that multiplicatively approximate the corresponding partition functions at fixed rational parameters throughout this regime, with high probability over the graph. Our algorithmic guarantees significantly improve upon prior work and extend beyond the parameter ranges reached by previously known search algorithms. To establish our results, we first develop reinforced small subgraph conditioning, which resolves short-cycle fluctuations in the complex plane by lifting classical small subgraph conditioning on random regular graphs to random holomorphic functions. Second, we overcome the standard second-moment barrier caused by the exponential blowup of the bare second moment inside the replica-symmetric phase, by showing that replica symmetry implies a nonpositive Bethe functional and upgrading planted overlap concentration to an exponentially small bound, which enables a typical-graph truncation that controls complex second moments up to the replica symmetry breaking threshold. △ Less

    Using domain walls to benchmark fast quantum anneals

    Authors: Shushmi Chowdhury, Asa Hopkins, Nick Chancellor, Viv Kendon

    Abstract: …However, for chains with random imperfections, prior work [Chancellor et al., npj Quantum Inf. 8, 73, 2022] found that the distribution in a physical implementation corresponded to neither. Instead, the domain wall distribution forms a U-shaped profile arising from a classical model with random fields, where the combination of averaging over these fields an… ▽ More We consider the distribution of domain walls arising in ground states of frustrated 1-dimensional Ising spin chains with anti-parallel boundary spins. For ideal chains, a sine-squared particle-in-a-box distribution of domain walls is expected in the quantum setting, and a uniform distribution expected classically. However, for chains with random imperfections, prior work [Chancellor et al., npj Quantum Inf. 8, 73, 2022] found that the distribution in a physical implementation corresponded to neither. Instead, the domain wall distribution forms a U-shaped profile arising from a classical model with random fields, where the combination of averaging over these fields and thermal averaging produces the characteristic U-shape. With D-Wave's newer fast anneal feature, we access anneal times shorter than the coherence time and observe the expected coherent quantum behavior in the form of a sine-squared distribution. For the shortest available anneal time, the dynamics have not yet reached the sine-squared shape and instead display a different U-shape in the middle of the chains. This motivates using the extent of the domain wall distribution tail in this regime as a platform independent metric that provides a signature of both coherence time and noise levels. △ Less

    Accurate MRI-free PET Quantification and Reliability Assessment via CT-to-MRI Synthesis and Conformal Prediction

    Authors: Jungwoo Seo, Seung Yun Choi, Kun Ho Lee, Seun Jeon, Byoung Seok Ye, Jiook Cha

    Abstract: …anatomical parcellation and PET quantification, while deep-ensemble uncertainty was calibrated with conformal prediction to estimate a plausible range for the corresponding real MRI-based SUVR. On $^{11}$C-PiB PET from OASIS-3, the proposed method achieved the highest synthesis and parcellation fidelity among the compared methods. Mean cortical SUVR from the… ▽ More Standardized uptake value ratio (SUVR) quantification of amyloid PET commonly relies on a subject-specific T1-weighted MRI, yet a usable MRI is not always available. We therefore combined CT-to-MRI synthesis with conformal prediction for MRI-free PET quantification with reliability assessment. A slice-consistent Brownian Bridge Diffusion Model was strengthened with multi-dataset training and data-source conditioning to enrich the anatomical prior learned from MRI, and with expanded multi-slice CT context to exploit the through-plane continuity of subject-specific anatomy in CT. Synthetic MRI was used for FreeSurfer-based anatomical parcellation and PET quantification, while deep-ensemble uncertainty was calibrated with conformal prediction to estimate a plausible range for the corresponding real MRI-based SUVR. On $^{11}$C-PiB PET from OASIS-3, the proposed method achieved the highest synthesis and parcellation fidelity among the compared methods. Mean cortical SUVR from the proposed synthetic MRI agreed closely with the real MRI-based reference (MAE $0.016 \pm 0.015$, Pearson $r = 0.9987$) and yielded the same amyloid-positivity classification as the real MRI in all 87 test subjects at the prespecified cut-off. Conformal prediction achieved empirical coverage close to the prespecified levels, and at 90% target coverage, 85 of 87 subjects had prediction intervals that supported an unambiguous amyloid-positivity decision. Zero-shot evaluation on an independent external cohort showed that the proposed CT-to-MRI model could support PET quantification without retraining, although conformal calibration required target-cohort recalibration to recover the intended coverage. These findings support CT-to-MRI-based MRI-free PET quantification as a promising approach for providing accurate SUVR measurements and an explicit assessment of their reliability. △ Less

    A Native-Reference Coordinate Geometry for L2 Pronunciation Deviation Using Self-Supervised Speech Models

    Authors: Tina Raissi, Nhan Phan, Mikko Kurimo

    Abstract: …distances show negative Spearman correlations up to -0.5 with speaking proficiency, indicating that higher-proficiency speakers tend to have smaller distances to the corresponding native phone-class coordinates. ▽ More Self-supervised speech models encode rich phonetic information, but it remains unclear how to transform this information into interpretable metrics for second-language (L2) pronunciation assessment in spontaneous speech. We propose a native-reference coordinate geometry in which phone-class averages from native speech define a low-dimensional reference subspace, and L2 speech is evaluated by its distance to matching native phone-class coordinates. Unlike prior distance-based approaches, our method does not require parallel recordings with matched linguistic content or dedicated pronunciation labels. Across different self-supervised encoders and modeling choices, the resulting native-reference distances show negative Spearman correlations up to -0.5 with speaking proficiency, indicating that higher-proficiency speakers tend to have smaller distances to the corresponding native phone-class coordinates. △ Less

    Rural Readiness: Domain Generalization with Adversarially Chosen Domains

    Authors: Cynthia Dwork, Andisheh Ghasemi, Lunjia Hu

    Abstract: …source domains. By applying this insight to the meta-distributional setting, in which source domains are selected according to a distribution on distributions, we obtain a corresponding improvement over known sample complexity upper bounds. We also study the effect of data access to the target domain and show that a small labeled sample from the target domai… ▽ More Predictive models trained at well-resourced data-rich institutions often fail when deployed in new populations. Motivated by the goal of building clinical diagnosis and decision-making algorithms for data-scarce rural populations using the general knowledge learned from data-rich hospitals, we formulate the fundamental question of \emph{domain generalization with adversarially chosen domains}, which asks a learner to perform on any unseen target domain nearly as well as the worst hypothesis consistent with the evidence from the observed source domains. Assuming only that the hypothesis class contains a "universal" predictor with small error on all $k$ source domains, we characterize the sample complexity of this objective up to logarithmic factors. Letting $d$ denote the VC dimension of the hypothesis class, we find, somewhat surprisingly, that the sample complexity reduces significantly from $Ω(dk)$ to $O(d)$ under a small relaxation of the learning goal in which, roughly speaking, we allow the learned model to compete with hypotheses that perform well on 99% instead of 100% of the source domains. By applying this insight to the meta-distributional setting, in which source domains are selected according to a distribution on distributions, we obtain a corresponding improvement over known sample complexity upper bounds. We also study the effect of data access to the target domain and show that a small labeled sample from the target domain does not improve the worst-case sample complexity on the source domains. △ Less

    PhysInsert: Physically Plausible Object Insertion with Geometric Priors and Bidirectional Object-Scene Interactions

    Abstract: We present PhysInsert, a framework for physically plausible reference-based object insertion that jointly adapts objects and their surrounding scenes. Existing methods often rely on masked inputs, generation-based pairs, or training pairs with limited viewpoint variation, leaving pose and illumination adaptation and bidirectional object-scene interactions insufficiently constrained. We construct t… ▽ More We present PhysInsert, a framework for physically plausible reference-based object insertion that jointly adapts objects and their surrounding scenes. Existing methods often rely on masked inputs, generation-based pairs, or training pairs with limited viewpoint variation, leaving pose and illumination adaptation and bidirectional object-scene interactions insufficiently constrained. We construct the Insert World dataset with strictly aligned Blender-rendered counterfactual pairs and object references across viewpoints and lighting conditions. Building on these data, Geometry-Guided Adaptation couples a VGGT geometry stream with image and text streams under target-scene feature supervision to guide spatially consistent insertion. Complementary 2D Object-Scene Spatial Supervision jointly supervises object occupancy and environmental changes while grounding spatial instructions in object regions through image-text attention. We introduce PhysInsert-Bench with dedicated cross-environment and cross-view cases requiring illumination and pose adaptation beyond appearance copying. Experiments on PhysInsert-Bench and DreamBooth demonstrate improved image fidelity; a user study further shows more accurate placement and improved lighting integration, perspective, and pose plausibility. △ Less

    Trajectory sharpening under quantum reference frame transformations

    Authors: Matteo Fadel, Giulia Gubitosi

Here we show that the trajectory-sharpening effect obtained by correlating a probed system with an effective negative-mass reference admits a natural interpretation as a QRF transformation to the frame of a negative-mass system. Using a minimal toy model of free particles with signed masses, we identify the Einstein-Podolsky-Rosen-type observables responsible for this effect with a pair of commuting relational observables that, under the instantaneous QRF transformation, are mapped to operators local to distinct subsystems. We then show that the corresponding relational trajectory is reproduced by the dynamical QRF transformation, where the transformed relational position acquires an explicit time dependence. We introduce QRF-invariant sharpness measures for relational trajectories and derive monogamy relations showing that a trajectory cannot be made simultaneously arbitrarily sharp with respect to two distinct negative-mass frames. We further show that trajectory sharpness can instead reside in a collective reference mode encoded across two physical systems, even when neither constituent alone provides an arbitrarily sharp reference, so that sharpness can be distributed rather than localized in a single constituent frame. These results provide an operational, experimentally relevant interpretation of QRF transformations for continuous-variable systems. △ Less

    Seeding and Amplification of Chaos in Long-Range Interacting Spin Chains

    Authors: Sareen Sabra, Jeffrey G. Rau

    Abstract: …, is recovered within the accessible space-time window. For power-law interactions, the arrival time is logarithmic in distance for all exponents studied, corresponding to an exponentially expanding spatial front; the power-law exponent controls the coefficient of this logarithm. We interpret both behaviors using a seeding-and-amplification picture in which… ▽ More Truncating weak interactions is a natural simplification of a many-body Hamiltonian, but the validity of this approximation need not be uniform in distance and time. In a chaotic system, a weak long-range coupling can seed a small disturbance far from its source, after which the local dynamics can amplify that disturbance exponentially. We study this mechanism in an infinite-temperature classical Heisenberg spin chain with a dominant nearest-neighbor exchange and a weak interaction tail. Using a classical decorrelator, we compare exponentially and power-law decaying tails over several decades in interaction strength. For exponentially decaying interactions, the long-distance front remains ballistic when the tail is sufficiently shallow, with a velocity controlled by the competition between the spatial decay of the seed and the local chaotic growth. For sufficiently rapid exponential decay, the nearest-neighbor front, which is defined by an intrinsic butterfly velocity $v_b$, is recovered within the accessible space-time window. For power-law interactions, the arrival time is logarithmic in distance for all exponents studied, corresponding to an exponentially expanding spatial front; the power-law exponent controls the coefficient of this logarithm. We interpret both behaviors using a seeding-and-amplification picture in which long-range couplings generate weak disturbances throughout the chain whose growth is governed primarily by the local nearest-neighbor dynamics. In this picture, the overall strength of the tail primarily controls when the long-range front becomes visible, while its spatial decay controls the propagation law. Thus a weak interaction tail can leave conventional spin-dynamical observables essentially unchanged while qualitatively altering the long-distance propagation of decorrelation. △ Less

    Authors: Claudio Serpico, Marco Lembo, Giovanni Miano

    Abstract: …and cavity fields are expanded in the normal modes of the uncoupled magnetic and electromagnetic subsystems, with coupling coefficients given by overlap integrals of the corresponding eigenfunctions. The resulting multimode Lagrangian has a noncanonical structure due to the gyrotropic terms, and the corresponding Hamil… ▽ More We investigate magnon-photon interactions in a resonant cavity starting from the coupled Maxwell and Landau-Lifshitz equations linearized about an arbitrary micromagnetic ground state. By introducing an electric vector potential and a suitable interaction Lagrangian, the formulation avoids the cancellation of large magnetostatic contributions that arises in the conventional magnetic-vector-potential description and becomes inaccurate under modal truncation. The magnetization and cavity fields are expanded in the normal modes of the uncoupled magnetic and electromagnetic subsystems, with coupling coefficients given by overlap integrals of the corresponding eigenfunctions. The resulting multimode Lagrangian has a noncanonical structure due to the gyrotropic terms, and the corresponding Hamiltonian formulation is obtained using the Faddeev-Jackiw approach. The radiative magnon-photon interaction is encoded in the resulting Poisson-bracket structure, while the cavity-induced modification of the magnetostatic field enters explicitly in the Hamiltonian. The coupled system is diagonalized through a generalized Bogoliubov transformation, yielding canonical polariton variables that provide a natural basis for quantization. Application to a perpendicularly magnetized YIG disk in a rectangular microwave cavity shows hybridization between the cavity mode and several magnetostatic modes, together with appreciable cavity-wall-induced frequency shifts when the sample is placed close to the conducting walls. △ Less

    Classical Simulation and Design Frontiers for IBM's Doped Clifford Sampling Experiment

    Authors: Hidetaka Manabe, Hanfeng Gu, Feng Pan

    Abstract: …complex64 entries (256 times smaller than IBM's estimation), corresponding to a tensor payload of $256$ GiB, and is distributed across eight GPUs within a node. Using 32 nodes, with eight NVIDIA H100 GPUs per node, we completed all 2051 amplitude batches corresponding to IBM's published output bitstrings in 37… ▽ More We classically simulate the IBM doped Clifford random circuit sampling experiment, comprising $70$ qubits, $70$ entangling layers, and $468$ inserted $T$ gates, as reported in the first version of arXiv:2607.25941. A deterministic temporal-boundary tensor network contraction approach is specifically designed to tackle such open-boundary one-dimensional brickwork circuits with operator-Schmidt-rank-$2$ entangling gates. For an $n$-qubit circuit of depth $d$, the resulting unsliced path evaluates an exact amplitude with contraction width $\lceil d/2\rceil$; Ratcatcher calculations certify that no smaller width is possible for the tested instances. Because one-qubit gates are absorbed without changing the network topology, the width and dense scheduled contraction cost are independent of their values and of the number and placement of $T$ gates. For this initial instance, its largest intermediate tensor contains $2^{35}$ complex64 entries (256 times smaller than IBM's estimation), corresponding to a tensor payload of $256$ GiB, and is distributed across eight GPUs within a node. Using 32 nodes, with eight NVIDIA H100 GPUs per node, we completed all 2051 amplitude batches corresponding to IBM's published output bitstrings in 37.3 minutes. The resulting probabilities yield a log-XEB estimate of $0.35034$ with a 95% interval of $[0.29763,0.40305]$. Under the Porter--Thomas and scrambled-noise assumptions, this is numerically compatible with IBM's fidelity lower bound; separately, fidelity-weighted resource accounting projects a 583-contraction workload with a 10.6-minute makespan on the same 32 nodes. More broadly, the approach provides a practical diagnostic for experimental outputs and a quantitative tool for designing future doped Clifford sampling experiments. △ Less

    OCCAMS: A Multi-Task Learning-Based Investigation of the CMB Power Spectra

    Authors: Ankit Mandal, Rahul Shah, Md. Wali Hossain, Somasri Sen, Anjan A Sen, Supratik Pal

    Abstract: …, AIC, BIC and Bayesian evidence. Their correspondence with cosmological parameters is measured with normalised mutual information post-training. The loss weights are set within a single training run by uncertainty weighting, with the weight of the disentanglement term either fixed (semi-automated, SA) or learned (completely automated, CA). The reconstructio… ▽ More How many latent variables are needed to fit the cosmic microwave background (CMB) temperature and polarisation power spectra when a neural network is given no cosmological model, and to what extent do they map onto the standard cosmological parameters? We use multi-task learning with variational autoencoders trained on CLASS-generated spectra (TT, TE, EE) over $2\le\ell\le2500$. No cosmological model is built into the network, and the number of latent variables, $L$, is varied over $L=5$, $6$ and $7$. Their fits are compared to the Planck 2018 data using $\hatχ^2$, AIC, BIC and Bayesian evidence. Their correspondence with cosmological parameters is measured with normalised mutual information post-training. The loss weights are set within a single training run by uncertainty weighting, with the weight of the disentanglement term either fixed (semi-automated, SA) or learned (completely automated, CA). The reconstruction loss is split over multipole ranges in three configurations that separate the roles of temperature and polarisation and of the low- and high-$\ell$ ranges. In four of the six combinations of configuration and strategy, the fit improves from $L=5$ to $L=6$ and not beyond, and $L=6$ is the best, or close to the best, in five of the six cases. So, six latent variables are needed, as many as the free parameters of the standard, model-dependent approach. SA gives clearer disentanglement, while CA needs no a priori choice of the disentanglement weight and has the higher evidence at $L=5$ and $6$: the two strategies are points on a trade-off between interpretability and fit to the data. Notably, the latent variables for $τ$ and $A_s$ share almost no information in any model, consistent with large-scale polarisation separating the two, as in standard analyses. A deep-learning approach can thus give a compact, data-driven parametrisation of the CMB spectra. △ Less

However, for established researchers, such pivots are associated with fewer publications and citations. Here, we investigate pivoting at the beginning of a research career--doctoral admissions--when the benefits of importing ideas might outweigh the switching costs.     Authors: Federico Gerbino, Donghoon Kim, Guido Giachetti, Andrea De Luca, Xhek Turkeshi

    Abstract: …We present two toy models that reveal two complementary visions and provide quantitative access to universal scaling: i) a discrete-time dynamic in which each time step corresponds to multiplication by a Gaussian random matrix; ii) weak continuous-time monitoring that induces a Dyson brownian motion of the eigenvalues of the density matrix. The first approa… ▽ More We study purification dynamics in monitored quantum processes governed by ensembles of quantum circuits in different random-matrix symmetry classes. We analyze the universal aspects that emerge away from the measurement induced phase transition and inside the volume/weak measurement phase and in the scaling limit of large time and Hilbert space dimension. We present two toy models that reveal two complementary visions and provide quantitative access to universal scaling: i) a discrete-time dynamic in which each time step corresponds to multiplication by a Gaussian random matrix; ii) weak continuous-time monitoring that induces a Dyson brownian motion of the eigenvalues of the density matrix. The first approach provides an algebraic characterization based on rotational invariance emerging in Kraus's operator space, focusing in particular on the unitary and orthogonal cases, respectively $β=2$ and $β=1$, with $β$ the Dyson random-matrix index. The second approach, on the other hand, allows for a unified treatment for any $β$, thanks to the mapping of the Fokker-Planck evolution of eigenvalues onto the Calogero-Sutherland integrable Hamiltonian diagonalized in terms of Jack polynomials. We provide explicit expressions for the universal decrease of Rényi entropies. We show that, approaching the universal scaling limit, numerical simulations of different models agree with each other and with our theoretical predictions. Our results clarify the existence of different classes of universality for the purification process in hybrid quantum systems, accessible in random circuit architectures and weak measurement protocols. △ Less

    A Unified Complexity Framework for Quantum Property Testing

    Authors: Qisheng Wang

    Abstract: …, combining Haar-random moment encoding with moment matching and best polynomial approximation. Corresponding query complexity lower bounds follow from quantum sample-to-query lifting. The framework yields (nearly) tight bounds for a broad class of problems, including entropy estimation (von Neumann, Rényi, and Tsallis), closeness estimation (trace distanc… ▽ More We develop a unified framework for analyzing the complexity of quantum property testing through functionals of the form $\mathcal{L}_φ(ρ) = \operatorname{tr}(φ(dρ))/d$, where $ρ$ is an unknown $d$-dimensional quantum state and $φ$ is a given function. A master theorem is established that derives sample complexity lower bounds for estimating $\mathcal{L}_φ(ρ)$ from properties of $φ$, combining Haar-random moment encoding with moment matching and best polynomial approximation. Corresponding query complexity lower bounds follow from quantum sample-to-query lifting. The framework yields (nearly) tight bounds for a broad class of problems, including entropy estimation (von Neumann, Rényi, and Tsallis), closeness estimation (trace distance and Uhlmann fidelity), spectrum estimation, rank testing (operator rank, Schmidt rank, and matrix product states). In particular, combined with known upper bounds, we show that the sample complexity of von Neumann entropy estimation is \[ Θ\!\left(\frac{d^2}{\varepsilon\log^2(d)\max\{1, \varepsilon\log^2(d)\}} + \frac{\log^2(d)}{\varepsilon^2}\right), \] and the sample complexity of spectrum estimation is \[ Θ\!\left(\frac{d^2}{\varepsilon^2\log^2(d)\max\{1, \varepsilon^2\log^2(d)\}}\right). \] In summary, our results resolve several open problems and establish the optimality of 37 quantum algorithms since 2015, up to constant or polylogarithmic factors. △ Less

Depending on the background of developers and users, conflicts between different goals may arise. While several, e.g., scripting languages allow a low barrier and user-friendly entry to mathematical modeling and simulation, corresponding implementations will most likely not allow timely outcomes for detailed simulations. In infectious disease modeling, while several large-scale software frameworks rely on C or C++ implementations, most models are implemented in Python or R. While the latter allow for fast prototyping and more interactive development, it has been shown for several applications that C or C++ implementations outperform plain Python or R implementations by factors between one and three orders of magnitude. In particular, in times of pandemics, the urgent need for swift estimation of the system's dynamics makes the use of large-scale models in these noncompiled languages prohibitive. In order to create synergies and close the gap between scientists from the application and the computational domain, binding libraries allow to execute entire, compiled software frameworks from languages such as Python. However, with iterative model development, extension, or addition of new models, implementing bindings can represent another relevant barrier to developers of the C or C++ backends. In this paper, we show how a large-scale software framework with a multitude of infectious disease models can be efficiently exposed to Python. In addition, we suggest a semi-automated way to generate interface code by analyzing generic C++ code through abstract syntax trees, enabling a modularized approach working with intermediate representations. △ Less

However, recent work shows that larger critics are prone to overfitting and instability in replay-based bootstrapped training. In this paper, we propose using Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) as a structural regularizer for critic learning from scratch. Empirically, LoRA reduces critic loss during training and improves overall policy performance, achieving the best or competitive results on most tasks. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our low-rank updates provide a simple and effective form of structural regularization for critic learning in off-policy RL. △ Less

    A Machian wave effect in conformal, scalar-tensor gravitational theory

    Authors: José Rodal

    Abstract: …driving and boundary data, the complete continuum conformal HN action becomes the Einstein action. Under these hypotheses, the additional measured force relative to the corresponding Einstein result is identically zero for the same physical matter model. Compatible global source-integral and absorber conditions restrict the admitted solutions and preserve t… ▽ More Woodward's simultaneous energy-density identities, imposed exactly with a constant physical speed of light and nonzero proper density, fix the potential and exclude the independent potential derivatives used in his derivation. A background interpretation instead requires the perturbation terms omitted by that derivation. In general relativity (GR), the second time derivative isolated from the exact Landau-Lifshitz equations belongs to the gravitational operator and supplies no additional material mass source. In the Hoyle-Narlikar (HN) derivation of Woodward and Fearn, the linear second time derivative cancels from the time-time component; the covariant trace cancels the squared-gradient terms retained in Fearn's diagonal sum. Their propulsion estimates contain further errors in mechanical power, momentum transfer and constitutive dimensions. The mechanical analysis retains damping, complex harmonic response and the dissipative energy and entropy balances. For a smooth strictly positive scalar, an admissible Weyl transformation, universal material coupling and consistently mapped matter, driving and boundary data, the complete continuum conformal HN action becomes the Einstein action. Under these hypotheses, the additional measured force relative to the corresponding Einstein result is identically zero for the same physical matter model. Compatible global source-integral and absorber conditions restrict the admitted solutions and preserve this equality. Woodward and Fearn's mass-fluctuation and propulsion formulas do not follow from the stated GR or continuum conformal HN equations. Their additional scalar propulsion force is excluded in the local continuum sector under the stated hypotheses. △ Less

The underlying geometry incorporates a quantum-correction parameter $q_c$ that alters the radial metric scaling while maintaining both asymptotic flatness and the classical horizon radius. The bound geodesics are systematically classified using the zoom--whirl representation labelled by three integers $(z,w,v)$. Gravitational waveforms are computed within a numerical framework that combines exact geodesic motion with the quadrupole approximation, which is suitable for extreme mass ratio inspirals. We demonstrate that the quantum corrections lead to distinct phase shifts, amplitude variations, and modifications to the harmonic structure of the waveforms, with increasingly complex features for orbits with larger zoom numbers. The corresponding frequency spectra and characteristic strain peak, which fall within the millihertz band, are within the sensitivity ranges of space-based detectors such as LISA, Taiji, and TianQin. For specific orbital configurations and values of the quantum-correction parameter, the characteristic strain exceeds the projected detector noise, indicating potential observability. Our results demonstrate that gravitational waves from periodic orbits provide a sensitive probe of quantum-corrected black hole spacetimes in the strong-field regime. △ Less

    Authors: Christopher Sinkule, Igor Zelenko

    Abstract: …pairs; in particular, the distribution admits a product structure, the squares of its components are involutive, and the restrictions of the two metrics to each leaf of the corresponding foliation are conformal to each other under the appropriate pullback. The proof is based on a new graph-theoretic formalism, in which compatible moving frames are represente… ▽ More This paper extends the classical results of Eisenhart (1923) and Levi-Civita (1896) on the classification of pairs of germs of Riemannian metrics with the same geodesics, either as parametrized curves (affine equivalence) or as unparametrized curves (projective equivalence), to sub-Riemannian metrics on arbitrary step 2 distributions: at every stable point in the projective case and at every point in the affine case. This extends the results of Zelenko (2004) for contact and even-contact distributions and the affine result of Lin and Zelenko (2024) for step 2 distributions with ad-surjective Tanaka symbols; our projective result covers the ad-surjective case as well. In the affine case, we prove that if a sub-Riemannian metric admits an affinely equivalent metric that is not constantly proportional to it, then locally it is a product of two sub-Riemannian metrics. In the projective case, we prove the 2019 conjecture of Jean, Maslovskaya, and Zelenko on generalized Levi-Civita pairs; in particular, the distribution admits a product structure, the squares of its components are involutive, and the restrictions of the two metrics to each leaf of the corresponding foliation are conformal to each other under the appropriate pullback. The proof is based on a new graph-theoretic formalism, in which compatible moving frames are represented by the vertices and edges of certain graphs. As a byproduct, we introduce a new discrete invariant of step 2 nilpotent graded Lie algebras, the characteristic tuple. The compatible graphs are used on the open dense set where this tuple is locally constant; the resulting bracket relations are identities between smooth tensors, so they extend by continuity across the points where the tuple jumps. Consequently, our classification holds without any assumption of local constancy of the characteristic tuple. △ Less

Surv., 2004], leading to an approximation algorithm of ratio $\frac {3k-1}4$ for odd $k \ge 3$ and of ratio $\frac {3k}4$ for even $k \ge 4$.     Authors: André Belotto da Silva, Avner Kiro

    Abstract: …approach the real plane. We also construct quasianalytic weight sequences with prescribed growth thresholds and establish corresponding counterexamples to exact division. More precisely, we construct quasianalytic Denjoy-Carleman classes $C^M$ whose local rings $C^M(\mathbb{R}^d,0)$ are not Noetherian for any $d\geq 2$. Herein, we propose a structure-informed deep Q-network (SI-DQN) that uses existing MS/MS evidence to decide whether further characterization is needed, which measurement to request, and when to stop. The policy combines spectral representations and candidate rankings with score-weighted descriptors of the candidates' chemical features and structural differences. The policy was evaluated on 79,462 held-out samples with simulated MS/MS, infrared (IR), and nuclear magnetic resonance (NMR) spectra from the Multimodal Spectroscopic Dataset. Evaluation used fixed hard candidate sets containing the correct structure and equal normalized acquisition costs. Averaged across three training runs, SI-DQN increased Top-1 accuracy from 80.10% to 93.78% for positive-ion MS/MS starts and from 70.44% to 93.31% for negative-ion starts. The corresponding mean additional measurement counts were only 0.227 and 0.340 per sample, respectively. Complete acquisition achieved 97.72% accuracy with four additions, whereas SI-DQN reduced the additional measurement count by 94.3% and 91.5%. Relative to a rule-based policy, SI-DQN reduced the composite loss combining terminal ranking error and acquisition cost by 19.3% and 25.5%. Adding structural information to DQN further improved accuracy while reducing requests across all three training runs. At comparable ranking uncertainty, SI-DQN increased request rates relative to DQN more strongly in groups with high structural disagreement, where larger reductions in composite loss were also observed. Together, these results support using the ambiguity among candidate structures after MS/MS analysis to guide further characterization, providing a quantitative basis for balancing identification performance against subsequent measurement effort. △ Less

    Cascade models of anisotropic turbulence in magnetized plasma of solar wind

    Authors: A. Bershadskii

    Abstract: …kinetic spectra. For switchbacks inside the upper solar corona, the corresponding large-scale spectra instead decouple into $k^{-2/3}$ magnetic and $k^{-5/3}$ kinetic spectra. Data from Voyager, Ulysses, Helios, Wind, and PSP confirm the spectral signatures across heliospheric regions spanning heliocentric distances from 0.056 to 140 AU. ▽ More The solar wind plasma is modeled as a collection of discrete domains, each possessing local rotational symmetry about its domain-mean magnetic field. Based on this partial symmetry, we construct cascade models governed by the nonlinear and Alfvénic time scales. We introduce a stochastic renewal process with a bimodal waiting-time distribution determined by these two time scales. This process yields a two-branch renormalization solution for the total energy: a statistically robust branch with an Iroshnikov-Kraichnan-like $k^{-3/2}$ spectrum and a statistically marginal branch with a Kolmogorov-like $k^{-5/3}$ spectrum. Whenever energy-flux fluctuations become supercritical, the system selects the faster of the two available cascade rates, thereby preventing flux accumulation. During solar wind expansion, the system undergoes a dynamical first-order phase transition from the slow $k^{-3/2}$ cascade to the fast $k^{-5/3}$ cascade, with switchbacks accelerating the transition. The mixing of subcritical and supercritical domains in raw spacecraft measurements produces an apparent decoupling of the velocity and magnetic-field spectral indices. Incorporating a magnetic helicity cascade slaved to the energy cascade, we show that the large-scale spectra decouple into $k^{-3/4}$ magnetic and $k^{-3/2}$ kinetic spectra. For switchbacks inside the upper solar corona, the corresponding large-scale spectra instead decouple into $k^{-2/3}$ magnetic and $k^{-5/3}$ kinetic spectra. Data from Voyager, Ulysses, Helios, Wind, and PSP confirm the spectral signatures across heliospheric regions spanning heliocentric distances from 0.056 to 140 AU. △ Less

These findings show how reward design shapes overcompetition… ▽ More Rapid advances in large language models (LLMs) have enabled autonomous agents to interact, coordinate, and pursue individual goals in artificial societies. However, our understanding of how reward structures shape their competitive behavior and collective outcomes remains incomplete. We simulate 200 LLM agents competing over 50 rounds to maximize individual wealth. We define overcompetition and lying flat as population investment above and below a Bayesian adaptive baseline, respectively. At the lowest tested reward, populations lie flat; at the two highest, they overcompete, most strongly at intermediate winner coverage. The investment committed to pursuing an uncertain reward constitutes the price of a chance. Agents near the qualification cutoff can invest heavily yet receive no prize, finishing poorer than nonwinners who invest less. Agents' stated rationales link investment decisions to perceived qualification prospects and expected returns. At the collective level, greater investment does not consistently improve ability selection. With a fixed total prize budget, offering smaller prizes to more winners can reduce investment while preserving selection quality relative to the corresponding top-ability target. Further broadening reward coverage yields additional savings but reduces selection quality. These findings show how reward design shapes overcompetition and lying flat, distributes the costs of pursuing opportunity, and influences what competition collectively achieves. △ Less

For $p=2$, we prove that nondegenerate stationary points are isolated and that the number of global min… ▽ More We study an optimization inverse spectral problem (OISP) for a one-dimensional Sturm--Liouville operator on a finite interval. The problem is to find a potential $V \in L^p(0,1)$ that realizes a prescribed finite set $λ_1^* < λ_2^* < \dots < λ_m^*$ and has the smallest $L^p(0,1)$-distance from an a priori given potential $V_0 \in L^p(0,1)$. A separate section provides examples with at least two global minima for $m=1,2$ in the presence of a sufficiently high finite symmetric barrier. We describe an iteration with frozen eigenfunctions and a local Newton--KKT method with continuation in the spectral data, and state explicitly the limits of their guarantees. The final part discusses applications of OISP theory to nonlinear boundary value problems and to applied problems of diagnostics and optimal spectral design of acoustic and electromagnetic resonators. △ Less

In this work, we study three representative entropic models, denoted as Models A, B, and C, corresponding to the driving terms $H^2$, $H^2+\dot H$, and $H^2+\dot H+H^4$, respectively. Model C includes more correction terms, but does not provide a better fit to the data.     Authors: Yvann Le Fay, Nicolas Chopin, Simon Barthelmé

    Abstract: …is exponential, the best approximation is the solution of a fixed-point equation. We show that LSVI is equivalent to stochasti… ▽ More Variational inference consists in finding the best approximation of a target distribution within a certain family, where `best' means (typically) smallest Kullback-Leiber divergence. We show that, when the approximation family is exponential, the best approximation is the solution of a fixed-point equation. We introduce LSVI (Least-Squares Variational Inference), a Monte Carlo variant of the corresponding fixed-point recursion, where each iteration boils down to ordinary least squares regression and does not require computing gradients. We show that LSVI is equivalent to stochastic mirror descent; we use this insight to derive convergence guarantees. We introduce various ideas to improve LSVI further when the approximation family is Gaussian, leading to a $O(d^3)$ complexity in the dimension $d$ of the target in the full-covariance case, and a $O(d)$ complexity in the mean-field case. We show that LSVI outperforms state-of-the-art methods in a range of examples, while remaining gradient-free, that is, it does not require computing gradients. △ Less

    Inevitability of the Petz transpose map

    Authors: Jacopo Surace, Chenxu Li, Francesco Buscemi

    Abstract: Bayes' rule is central to classical statistical inference: given a prior and a stochastic model, it determines the corresponding retrodictive conditional probabilities. In quantum theory, however, the question of whether an equally general rule of retrodiction exists and, if so, whether it is uniquely determined remains nontrivial. Here we prove that a r… ▽ More Bayes' rule is central to classical statistical inference: given a prior and a stochastic model, it determines the corresponding retrodictive conditional probabilities. In quantum theory, however, the question of whether an equally general rule of retrodiction exists and, if so, whether it is uniquely determined remains nontrivial. Here we prove that a retrodiction assignment defined for all priors and channels, which takes each predicted output state back to its prior, must coincide with the Petz transpose map if it is involutive, respects sequential and parallel composition, and agrees with unitary inversion. The theorem holds for channels between finite-dimensional full matrix algebras, with the input and output spaces restricted to the supports of the corresponding reference states. In particular, affinity under probabilistic mixtures and the characteristic square-root dependence on the reference states follow from these requirements rather than being postulated. Without involutivity, the remaining requirements characterize a family of doubly rotated Petz maps. Our result thus explains why the Petz transpose map arises in seemingly different areas of quantum theory: it is the unique quantum analogue of Bayes' rule compatible with these consistency requirements. △ Less

    math.ST stat.ME

    Graphical Models Induced by Irreversible Event Accumulation on Trees

    Authors: Rodrigo Labouriau

    Abstract: …replaced by a junction tree. The conditional independence graph of the event indicators is always decomposable and, in fact, is the line graph of the tree (maximal cliques correspond to nodes where two or more edges meet and separators are the single events on the edges joining two such nodes). The graph determines the tree up to the choice of root. A conn… ▽ More Decomposable graphical models are usually adopted for their computational convenience rather than derived from a data-generating mechanism. We show that a special class of them arises naturally from the irreversible accumulation of events along a branching history. Each event is attached to an edge of a latent rooted tree and is inherited by everything below that edge; an observation records which events have occurred at a randomly chosen node, not necessarily a leaf. When the tree is a path, this is the cumulative coding of an ordinal variable, whose indicators form a Markov chain; in the general tree case the Markov chain is replaced by a junction tree. The conditional independence graph of the event indicators is always decomposable and, in fact, is the line graph of the tree (maximal cliques correspond to nodes where two or more edges meet and separators are the single events on the edges joining two such nodes). The graph determines the tree up to the choice of root. A connected graph arises in this way if, and only if, it is chordal, any two of its maximal cliques share at most one vertex, no vertex is in three maximal cliques, and the graph is such that any two maximal cliques share at most one vertex. This provides a simple graphical test for compatibility with the mechanism (necessary but not sufficient, as irreversibility also constrains the distribution's support). The structure breaks down if we look at just the leaves, and measurement error destroys the exact screening and leads to a denser graph rather than simply a weaker dependence. We discuss inference, provide diagnostics for departures such as recurrent events, illustrate them on some simulated data and discuss their application in the context of mutation accumulation and lineage tracing where both ancestral and descendant types are sampled together. △ Less

We prove that defectiveness in the spatial transfer matrix enforc… ▽ More Spatial wave localization at boundaries is conventionally restricted to exponential decay. Here, we observe a scale-free boundary mode exhibiting anomalous polynomial decay, highlighted by strictly linear spatial envelopes, driven by topological multicriticality in acoustic metamaterials. We establish a universal geometric bulk-boundary correspondence rooted in singularity theory by mapping n-th order topological multicriticality onto Arnold An catastrophe. We prove that defectiveness in the spatial transfer matrix enforces a (n-1)-th power spatial decay of boundary modes. At a topological tri-critical point, the singularity of winding function manifests as an A2 fold catastrophe. This dictates a strictly linear spatial decay, marking a sharp departure from the conventional exponential scaling paradigm. Using acoustic metamaterial platforms tuned to a tricritical point, we experimentally demonstrate this linear decay envelope in 1D, and further generalize it to two-dimensional systems to realize scale-free corner modes with simultaneous linear decay along orthogonal directions. While existing critical topology frameworks rely on entanglement spectra to diagnose mode existence in gapless regimes, our work bridges catastrophe theory with topological matter at multicriticalities, advancing the field from abstract mode identification to deterministic real-space wavefunction engineering. △ Less

Global AI weather models can produce skillful medium-range forecasts, but their native 0.25 degrees resolution limits direct use for local hazard applications. Statistical downscaling can help bridge this gap, yet existing approaches often struggle with state-dependent, and especially lead-time-dependent, biases in global forecasts. We introduce SwAIther-Precip, a lead-time-aware downscaling framework that converts coarse-resolution AIFS forecasts into probabilistic km-scale precipitation fields over Switzerland. First, a U-Net conditioned on lead time via feature-wise linear modulation deterministically corrects systematic biases at coarse resolution. This targeted correction enables a cheaper super-resolution stage conditioned only on corrected precipitation, allowing direct training on observations rather than on the full atmospheric state. Using AIFS forecasts and CombiPrecip radar-gauge observations, SwAIther-Precip reduces CRPS by 48% relative to raw AIFS. These results show that explicitly correcting lead-time-dependent biases before generative super-resolution is key to efficient km-scale probabilistic downscaling of global AI precipitation forecasts. △ Less

Existing approaches commonly use Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) to train a separate adapter for each domain and combine these adapters within one model. However, they either weight adapters equally regardless of the input domain or dynamically weight adapters while still activating all of them including irrelevant ones. In this paper, we propose Domain-Specialized Upcycling (DS-Upcycling) for multi-domain adaptation. Experiments show that our method achieves the highest average performance across the adapted domains compared to baselines while retaining general performance. An excess of events is observed over the baseline perturbative QCD prediction, with an observed significance over $8$ standard deviations.     A Sharp Joint Bias-Energy Envelope for Radial Clipping

    Authors: Danila Litvinov

    Abstract: …. This same constant remains sharp in the stochastic counterpart for random vectors in a Hilbert space. We also find the exact supremum of the corresponding linear combination of normalized bias and energy for arbitrary $α,β\in\mathbb R$. We then give a geometric picture in the energy-bias plane. Varying the weights, we describe the smallest closed convex re… ▽ More Radial clipping does two things at once: it cuts off the part of a vector outside a ball and leaves a bounded vector inside the ball. The part cut off defines the residual. For a random vector, we study the magnitude of the bias in the mean and the energy, defined as the centered second moment of the clipped vector. Controlling these effects is important in stochastic optimization with heavy tails, where the choice of the clipping threshold affects convergence bounds for algorithms. In the deterministic problem, for a fixed threshold $τ>0$, $1\le p\le2$, and nonnegative coefficients $α,β$, we find the smallest constant that simultaneously bounds the residual and energy in terms of $\lVert x\rVert^p$. This same constant remains sharp in the stochastic counterpart for random vectors in a Hilbert space. We also find the exact supremum of the corresponding linear combination of normalized bias and energy for arbitrary $α,β\in\mathbb R$. We then give a geometric picture in the energy-bias plane. Varying the weights, we describe the smallest closed convex region containing all normalized pairs and the attainable set itself, identifying unattainable boundary values. Every attainable pair at a fixed threshold can be realized by a real-valued random variable taking at most two values. We also consider all thresholds simultaneously. For any integrable random vector in a Hilbert space, we construct a single $\mathbb R^3$-valued random vector preserving its norm distribution, bias magnitude, and energy simultaneously at every threshold $τ>0$. Finally, we apply the joint bound to risk-adjusted reinsurance costs for a prescribed retention threshold and to the logarithmic shortfall of a clipped portfolio strategy relative to any constant-fraction strategy. AI was used in preparing this paper (see Section 8). △ Less

In particular, the best performing hybrid post VQC architecture correct… ▽ More Machine learning (ML) techniques are increasingly explored to analyse gravitational wave (GW) data expected from the Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (LISA). At the same time, advances in quantum computing have stimulated interest in quantum machine learning as a potential alternative to conventional approaches. We investigate the applicability of quantum, hybrid quantum-classical, and classical ML models to two tasks relevant for LISA observations of massive black hole binaries: GW signal detection and merger prediction. Using the Sangria dataset from the LISA Data Challenge, we benchmark several quantum architectures, including variational quantum classifiers (VQCs), post VQC hybrid models, quantum kernel methods, data reuploading classifiers, and quantum long short-term memory (QLSTM) networks, against classical support vector machines, multilayer perceptrons, recurrent neural networks, and LSTM networks. The models are trained on labelled data derived from the Sangria training dataset and evaluated on the corresponding blind dataset. For signal detection, several quantum models match or outperform classical baselines. In particular, the best performing hybrid post VQC architecture correctly identifies all signal samples in the blind dataset, while the standard VQC and data reuploading classifier achieve competitive detection rates. For merger detection and coalescence time estimation, however, the standard VQC emerges as the most reliable model, successfully localising all detected merger events with the lowest localisation errors. We further analyse the trade-offs between predictive performance, model complexity, and quantum circuit resources. Our results indicate that, although no universal quantum advantage is observed, selected quantum models can provide practical benefits for specific GW analysis tasks and represent promising candidates for future LISA data analysis pipelines. △ Less

    Authors: Bai-He Yao, Zhi-Yan Shao, Ye Cao, Fan Yang

    Abstract: …based classification of collinear magnetism in the quasicrystal (QC) to general magnetic states. Unlike conventional AM wherein zero magnetism is protected… ▽ More We generalize the previous group-theory based classification of collinear magnetism in the quasicrystal (QC) to general magnetic states. Taking the Penrose lattice as an example, we find that the magnetic states corresponding to 2D irreducible representations (IRRPs) of the $D_5$ point group represent for a new type of altermagnetism (AM). Unlike conventional AM wherein zero magnetism is protected by, e.g. rotation or mirror reflection, which globally flips the spin in the magnetic pattern, the zero magnetism in these 2D-IRRP states is instead protected by a special group-representation-orthogonality theorem which states that basis functions belonging to different IRRPs are mutually orthogonal. This special compensation mechanism and the absence of $\mathcal{PT}$ symmetry finally lead to AM states. Taking the Penrose-Hubbard model as an example, our linear-response theory and Ginzburg-Landau theory based analysis show that the largest portion of the parameter space is occupied by such 2D-IRRP states, which can be either collinear or coplanar AM states. These special AM states are characterized by spin split in the spin-resolved angular-resolved photoemission spectroscopy and the spin conductance with unique symmetry features. These intriguing collinear and coplanar AM states result from symmetry and can also emerge in other QCs. △ Less

    Optimizing Incomplete, Large-Scale and Sparse Multi-Graph Matching in Bioimaging

    Authors: Max Kahl, Sebastian Stricker, Lisa Hutschenreiter, Florian Bernard, Carsten Rother, Bogdan Savchynskyy

    Abstract: …in computer vision. Existing datasets do not cover this large-scale regime, and virtually all existing methods are inapplicable because they assume a complete or dense problem setting. To suppor… ▽ More Multi-graph matching is a fundamental problem in computer vision. Our work is motivated by a challenging application in bioimaging, where dozens or even hundreds of 3D microscopy images of worms must be brought into correspondence. Existing datasets do not cover this large-scale regime, and virtually all existing methods are inapplicable because they assume a complete or dense problem setting. To support further research, our first contribution is a new large-scale dataset based on problem instances from bioimaging. Our second contribution is a comprehensive analysis of the two main multi-graph matching paradigms: direct and permutation synchronization-based formulations. We argue, in part by proof, that practical large-scale methods must explicitly address problem sparsity and incompleteness. Since standard permutation synchronization approaches fail in this setting, we further introduce a sparse permutation synchronization paradigm. Our final contribution is GREEDA, a general method for sparse and incomplete problems that can be instantiated across cost orders and paradigms. While our paper focuses on objective functions up to quadratic order, GREEDA is inherently generalizable to arbitrary orders. On larger, sparse instances, GREEDA outperforms competing methods in both objective value and runtime. For example, for moderately sized problems based on 30 worm images GREEDA produces a high-quality solution within 2 minutes, whereas competitors require at least an hour and yield far worse results. On smaller dense problems, GREEDA remains on par with leading methods while being an order of magnitude faster. △ Less

However, positional fluctuations during optical confinement introduce spatial sampling errors that compromise imaging quality and acquisition speed. Here, we demonstrate a scattering-force-driven optical manipulation imaging method that replaces the stepwise scanning of conventional optical trapping with continuous directional transport of microparticles. An axial scattering force generated by a low-numerical-aperture focused beam propels microparticles through a light sheet, while their positions and fluorescence signals are simultaneously recorded to establish direct spatial correspondence between particle motion and tomographic images. Experiments demonstrate that this approach reduces the effects of positional fluctuations on 3D imaging, achieving an axial sampling interval of 0.1 μm and an imaging rate of 250 Hz. The method also enables effective imaging of samples with diverse structural features while reducing optical power requirements by 70%. By combining robust spatial sampling, high-speed acquisition and reduced photodamage, this approach offers a promising route to rapid 3D imaging, dynamic observation and microscale spatial analysis of microparticles and biological specimens. △ Less

On the other hand, we find that the endpoint Sobolev regularity also fails on a bounded segment of the reduced Newton polyhedron… ▽ More In this paper, we consider the endpoint Sobolev regularity of Radon transforms associated with real-analytic functions in the plane. We establish all of the failure of endpoint regularity for real-analytic phases. On the one hand, the critical endpoint regularity $L^p(\mathbb{R}^2)\rightarrow L^p_s(\mathbb{R}^2)$, corresponding to extreme points of the reduced Newton polyhedron of phase function, is true if only and only if $p=2$. For generic extreme points, this failure of endpoint regularity generalizes a result of Christ \cite{christ}. On the other hand, we find that the endpoint Sobolev regularity also fails on a bounded segment of the reduced Newton polyhedron when the bisectrix $y=x$ intersects one of unbounded faces at a relative interior point. In particular, our results imply that the Radon transform is not bounded from $L^p$ into $L^p_{1/n}$ with $p=n/(n-1)$ or $p=n$, where the phase functions are real-valued homogeneous polynomials $S(x,y)=a_1x^{n-1}y+a_2x^{n-2}y^2+\cdots+a_{n-1}xy^{n-1}$ with $n\geq 3$ and $a_1a_{n-1}\neq0$. This answers a problem of Phong and Stein in 1994. Moreover, the main results of this paper also give examples which show failure of critical Sobolev regularity for degenerate Fourier integral operators satisfying finite type conditions formulated in \cite{Seeger93,seeger2}. Based on earlier results and our proof of several remaining cases, we conclude that all of other critical endpoint Sobolev estimates are true for Radon transforms associated with real-analytic phases in the plane. Therefore, all failures of endpoint regularity estimates are clearly determined. △ Less

    Abelian Orbifolds for Brane Brick Models

    Authors: Juno Kwon, Rak-Kyeong Seong

    Abstract: We present a systematic procedure for constructing brane brick models corresponding to abelian orbifolds of toric Calabi-Yau 4-folds, extending the orbifold construction beyond the well-studied case of abelian orbifolds of C^4. Given a parent brane brick model… ▽ More We present a systematic procedure for constructing brane brick models corresponding to abelian orbifolds of toric Calabi-Yau 4-folds, extending the orbifold construction beyond the well-studied case of abelian orbifolds of C^4. Given a parent brane brick model corresponding to a toric Calabi-Yau 4-fold M, we show that the action of an abelian orbifold group Gamma on the generators of M induces an action on the chiral and Fermi fields as well as the J- and E-terms of the associated 2d (0,2) supersymmetric gauge theory. The requirement that the orbifolded brane brick model remains consistent with the closed paths associated with the J- and E-terms, together with the chiral cycles formed by their products, precisely reproduces the Calabi-Yau condition on the orbifold action. This procedure yields an explicit formula for the J- and E-terms of the orbifolded brane brick model in terms of those of the parent theory. We apply our construction to the brane brick models corresponding to Q^{1,1,1} and D_3, and present the resulting families of 2d (0,2) quiver gauge theories. An injection-and-recovery test demonstrates that a 1287 ppm transit signal corresponding to a minimum-radius $1.69 R_{\oplus}$ planet would have been detected with Signal-to-Pink-Noise Ratio SPNR $> 7$, ruling out a transiting geometry with high confidence. The orbital inclination is constrained to $i < 89.3^{\circ}$. With an incident stellar flux $S_{\rm eff} \approx 0.889 S_{\oplus}$ and bolometric luminosity $L_* = 0.0273 \pm 0.0023 L_{\odot}$, Gliese 48 b lies near the inner edge of the Conservative Habitable Zone and within the Optimistic HZ, making it one of the most astrobiologically compelling temperate Super-Earths orbiting an M-dwarf. △ Less
