However, prompt-tuned VLMs often suffer from a base-to-novel dual miscalibration problem, where models typically show underconfidence on base classes and overconfidence on novel classes. Current VLM calibration approaches address this dual miscalibration either separately or jointly, but lack a geometric understanding of the hyperspherical embedding space and thus fail to exploit angular discriminative properties to adjust distorted decision boundaries. To address this, we propose M-PFT, a novel angular-margin approach for prompt tuning that introduces two complementary objectives to promote class discriminability and semantic anchoring. This enlarges the angular margin by 1) minimizing the maximum cosine similarity between tunable class embeddings to reduce embedding collapse and 2) minimizing the angular distance between each tunable embedding and its corresponding zero-shot embedding to preserve semantic geometry. Extensive experiments across 9 prompt-tuning methods with various backbones on 15 diverse datasets show that our approach reduces the average calibration error on novel classes, while remaining competitive on base classes and maintaining comparable accuracy. Further analyses reveal that M-PFT effectively adjusts distorted decision boundaries induced by prompt tuning while preserving the pre-trained embedding space, improving calibration with negligible computational overhead. △ Less

We present TokWatt, an open database of 134 published estimates of AI inference energy drawn from 13 sources (2023-2026) and covering 20 tasks, from classic machine-learning inferences to chat answers, reasoning answers, long-document queries, image and video generation, and coding agents. Each estimate records its functional unit, five measurement-boundary components, method, reported interval, and the location of the value in its source. A reproducible procedure scales estimates to a common server-plus-facility boundary and derives per-task reference values with ranges. The reference values span six orders of magnitude: 0.34 Wh for a short chat answer (range 0.16-0.99 Wh), 4.7 Wh for a reasoning answer, 124 Wh for a short video clip, and 150 Wh for one task delegated to a coding agent. Five independent estimates for default production chat systems agree within a factor of 1.8, and the reference values shift by at most 12% under alternative harmonization parameters. A simple identity links personal use to grid load: E watt-hours per person per day across one billion people corresponds to an average draw of E/24 gigawatts. Data and code are released at https://github.com/AraoufBh/tokwatt, and an online calculator at https://araoufbh.github.io/tokwatt/. △ Less

    Unitary-Invariant Decomposition of Reducible Total Least Squares Core Problems

    Authors: Sijia Yu, Bruno Carpentieri, Yan-Fei Jing

    Abstract: …We split the data matrix into row blocks associated with the singular subspaces of the system matrix and consider the graph whose ver- tices are the blocks and whose edges correspond to nonzero cross-products. After preliminary splittings that make every nonzero cross-product a scalar multiple of a unitary matrix, we show that the components can be obtained… ▽ More The analysis of a total least squares problem can be reduced to that of an associated core problem, which typically has lower dimension and improved solubility properties. Nevertheless, even a core problem may remain reducible, admitting further decomposition into irreducible component sub- problems with simpler structure and better analytical properties. We seek the finest decomposition under unitary transformations over C; for real data this decomposition can be strictly finer than the finest one under real orthogonal transformations. We split the data matrix into row blocks associated with the singular subspaces of the system matrix and consider the graph whose ver- tices are the blocks and whose edges correspond to nonzero cross-products. After preliminary splittings that make every nonzero cross-product a scalar multiple of a unitary matrix, we show that the components can be obtained directly from these blocks if and only if the covariance matrices along all cycles of the graph are scalar matrices. Otherwise, there is a chordless cycle whose covariance matrix is not a scalar matrix, and splitting the blocks on this cy- cle along the eigenspaces of the covariance matrix removes the obstruction without changing the decomposition. Based on these results, we propose a recursive strategy that finds all irreducible components over the complex field. The strategy is exact and theoretical rather than an efficient numerical algo- rithm; a small floating-point computation illustrates it. The results depend only on the core problem and therefore apply to general linear approximation problems AX B. △ Less

    Authors: Emanuel Milman

In particular, we show that the Hilbert-Brunn-Minkowski operator coincides with the centro-affine Laplacian, thus obtaining a new avenue for tackling the conjecture using insights from affine differential geometry. As every strongly convex hypersurface in $\mathbb{R}^n$ is a centro-affine unit-sphere, it has constant centro-affine Ricci curvature equal to $n-2$, in stark contrast to the standard weighted Ricci curvature of the associated metric-measure space, which will in general be negative. As a consequence, we resolve the isomorphic version of the log-Minkowski problem: for any origin-symmetric convex body $\bar K$ in $\mathbb{R}^n$, there exists an origin-symmetric convex body $K$ with $\bar K \subset K \subset 8 \bar K$, so that $K$ satisfies the log-Minkowski conjectured inequality, and so that $K$ is uniquely determined by its cone-volume measure $V_K$. If $\bar K$ is not extremely far from a Euclidean ball to begin with, an analogous isometric result, where $8$ is replaced by $1+ε$, is obtained as well. △ Less

    Authors: Giovanni Russo, Ezequiel E. Ferrero, Alejandro B. Kolton, Alberto Rosso, Damien Vandembroucq

    Abstract: …, corresponds to the optimal activated rearrangements that control the dynamics' bottleneck and remains essentially temperature-independent. By combining structural and dynamical observables, we show that… ▽ More We investigate the creep dynamics of a driven elastic line at finite temperature, well below the depinning threshold. We show that creep is governed by two distinct length scales. The first, $\ell_{\mathrm{opt}}$, corresponds to the optimal activated rearrangements that control the dynamics' bottleneck and remains essentially temperature-independent. The second, $\ell_{\mathrm{av}}$, characterizes the spatial extent of thermally activated avalanches and grows as temperature decreases. By combining structural and dynamical observables, we show that $\ell_{\mathrm{av}}$ governs both the crossover in the structure factor and the growth of the four-point dynamical susceptibility, while the relaxation time remains controlled by activation over large barriers associated with $\ell_{\mathrm{opt}}$. We find that the avalanche scale follows $\ell_{\mathrm{av}}(T)\sim T^{-ν_{\mathrm{dep}}}$, thereby selecting a unique scenario among competing theoretical predictions. These results establish a unified picture of finite-temperature creep in which activation controls temporal scales while depinning criticality governs spatial correlations. △ Less

Both results hold over every field $E$ of characteristic $0$, for suitable $E$-rational structures on the two quivers; for $E=\mathbb{C}$ they recover Gelfand's equivalence. In this rational framework the defining relation of the Gelfand quiver acquires a descent-theoretic meaning: its Galois orbit is stable, it descends to a single relation on the associated $\mathbb{Q}$-species, and a $\mathbb{Q}$-rational representation satisfies it if and only if the composite of the two arrows through the vertex $-$ is a $\mathbb{Q}$-rational endomorphism of the space at the central vertex. This allows us to recast our results in the language of $\mathbb{Q}$-species. △ Less

We present Xamt, a cross-framework differential fuzzing approach for deep learning library APIs. It uses explicit API aliases and parameter-role normalization to construct candidate correspondences and validates them through pairwise execution and a group-level behavioral check on canonical ordinary inputs. The resulting groups are explored using variance-guided differential fuzzing with ordinary, boundary, and non-finite inputs. Crash and inconsistency oracles flag executions exhibiting abnormal termination or inconsistent outputs for subsequent reproduction and analysis. Across the seven libraries, Xamt constructs 676 execution-validated groups containing 2,563 matched APIs. Among these, Xamt identifies 72 independently reproduced discrepancy cases, including 4 crash cases and 68 output inconsistencies. Among the 72 developer reports, 25 have been confirmed, including 23 that have been fixed. △ Less

    Authors: Sreeshma A, Md Sabir Ali, C. L. Ahmed Rizwan

    Abstract: …theory and a complex residue approach. The winding numbers associated with these defects are utilized to calculate the black hole's global topological number. We begin with an a… ▽ More The interplay between black hole thermodynamics and topology provides a strong, coordinate-independent framework for discovering the underlying structure of black hole phase transitions as well as their universal features. We explore the thermodynamic topology of the anisotropic Kerr-Newman-AdS black hole using Duan's $φ$-mapping theory and a complex residue approach. By creating the generalized off-shell free energy, we propose a thermodynamic vector field in the parameter space with zero points corresponding to equilibrium black hole states. The winding numbers associated with these defects are utilized to calculate the black hole's global topological number. We begin with an analysis of the entire anisotropic Kerr-Newman-AdS black hole, followed by two limiting examples. Setting the anisotropic parameter $K = 0$ produces the Kerr-Newman-AdS black hole, while eliminating the AdS contribution produces the Kerr-Newman black hole. The anisotropic Kerr-Newman-AdS and Kerr-Newman-AdS black holes have a single thermodynamic defect with winding number $+1$, which results in $W = +1$. In contrast, the Kerr-Newman black hole has two defects with winding numbers $+1$ and $-1$, which result in a vanishing global topological number, $W = 0$. These findings show that the AdS contribution alters the global topological classification, but the anisotropic matter studied here preserves the topological class of the Kerr-Newman-AdS black hole. As an analogous limiting instance, we further study a Kiselev-type black hole, analyzing the circumstances in which its metric reduces to a charged black-hole geometry and how the thermodynamic topology reflects this reduction. Finally, we use the complex residue approach on the anisotropic Kerr-Newman-AdS black hole. △ Less

Existing coarse-to-fine methods use coarse matches to select regions for fine matching, but incorrect selection can remove valid correspondences before refinement. We introduce \paper, a conditional transport framework that connects coarse correspondence estimation to high-resolution matching. A diffusion model first estimates a coarse matching matrix. We show that this breaks the coexistence of dominant superconducting and density-density correlations decisivel… ▽ More Boosting superconductivity by metallic reservoirs is the essence of Kivelson's bilayer proposal. One layer provides pairing to the electrons, while the weakly coupled metal provides additional phase coherence to those pairs by mediating extended-range pair-pair coupling. Demonstrating significant and unambiguous performance gains with strong-coupling methods for such set-ups had been difficult. We show that this breaks the coexistence of dominant superconducting and density-density correlations decisively in favour or the former. Consequently, we provide evidence that in this doped regime, superconducting near-long-range order is not precluded by a small charge-gap in the thermodynamic limit, as we have recently shown to be the case at half-filling [J. E. Ebot \textit{et al.}, Phys. Rev. B \textbf{113}, 174530 (2026)]. We study the complex manner in which the enhancement of superconductivity in the pairing layer depends on the parameters of the metal, and especially that both pairing-limited and stiffness-limited regimes may appear in these systems. In addition to superconducting bilayers, our results are relevant, via a particle-hole transformation, for heavy-fermion Kondo-lattice materials in magnetic fields, as we provide previously lacking insight on the competition between antiferromagnetic and easy-plane magnetism, as well as a route for comprehensive indirect tests of Kivelson's bilayer proposal well beyond previous capabilities. △ Less

A complete garbled processor provides a fixed circuit for many programs, but its evaluator repeatedly visits processor-wide structures even when only a small part performs the current protected computation. We present RV2GC, which reconstructs the garbling boundary around a fixed library of RISC-V instruction circuits. Public scheduling and operand routing operate outside the circuit topologies; persistent label-encoded state connects independently invoked garbled instruction circuits and instruction-specific garbling-free paths through a common interface. A Conditional-execution Stack and protected commits support secret-dependent branches, bounded loops, and secret-indexed memory access under public scheduling, keeping instruction and memory-address sequences independent of private inputs. Across nine benchmarks, measured ARM2GC-to-RV2GC runtime ratios range from 12.81 to 4,043. In terms of circuit size, the largest listed instruction circuit has 3,386 gates, corresponding to 2.044% and 0.393% of the 256-byte and 4,096-byte Amber processors, respectively. Additional comparisons quantify schedule storage, communication, and the cost of secret-dependent execution. △ Less

On CAMUS at $128^2$ it retains the lowest error at increasing pose-perturbation magnitude. It holds $6.49$ M parameters independent of resolution, sustaining $51$ FPS at $512^2$. By coupling correspondence-based accuracy with bounded worst-case error and resolution-independent cost, SCoPE-Reg becomes viable at native acquisition resolution during intervention, whereas previous methods had to compromise on accuracy, reliability or frame rate.     Trajeform: Embedding Crowd Data

    Authors: Marilena Lemonari, Panayiotis Charalambous, Yiorgos Chrysanthou

    Abstract: …by simulators and used to compare the resulting embedding distributions. Additionally, the learned embedding can be used to analyse supplementary data, such as trajectories corresponding to a simulator's principal behaviours or captured data from controlled environments (e.g., cycling), providing insight into their ability to span the real behavioural em… ▽ More Crowd simulation aims to generate agent behaviours that are rich, natural, and appropriate to their surrounding environment. Recent advances in Machine Learning have enabled such behavioural patterns to be learned directly from trajectory data, allowing simulation models to capture increasingly complex crowd dynamics. However, the capabilities of these systems remain strongly dependent on the type and diversity of the data used during training. When evaluating how well crowd simulators generalise to real-world datasets, most existing metrics focus primarily on trajectory-level differences rather than behaviour-level similarity. In this paper, we present Trajeform, a framework that trains a reconstruction VAE (Variational Autoencoder) on real trajectory data to learn a meaningful continuous embedding that preserves behavioural characteristics of real crowds. The trained encoder can then be applied to synthetic trajectories generated by simulators and used to compare the resulting embedding distributions. Additionally, the learned embedding can be used to analyse supplementary data, such as trajectories corresponding to a simulator's principal behaviours or captured data from controlled environments (e.g., cycling), providing insight into their ability to span the real behavioural embedding space. The framework compares data from three simulators (CCP, modCCP, and RVO) against ground-truth datasets and is evaluated quantitatively and qualitatively using latent-space analysis, distributional metrics, and trajectory generation through sampling from the aggregated posterior distribution. The results indicate that the learned latent space captures meaningful trajectory dependencies found in the real data, making the framework useful both as an additional measure of synthetic data believability and as a tool for informing the training of future trajectory models. △ Less

Put $S_K=\{\mathfrak{P}:\mathfrak{P}\mid2\}$ and $S_K'=\{\mathfrak{q}:\mathfrak{q}\mid2ABC\}$. Both criteria use a valuation bound on the $S_K'$-unit equation at a fixed $\mathfrak{P}\in S_K$. In particular, it excludes non-trivial primitive integral solutions with $\mathfrak{P}\mid abc$. Neither criterion restricts the residue degree of $\mathfrak{P}$. Both criteria hold unconditionally over every totally real field; over general number fields, we assume residual modularity and Eichler--Shimura-type conjectures. The first criterion covers a new coefficient range. In particular, under the corresponding $S_K$-unit bound, $x^p+y^p=2^rz^p$ has no non-trivial solutions in $K^3$ for each fixed $r\geq5$ and sufficiently large prime $p$. This bound holds for a set of real quadratic fields of relative density $1$. For a family of imaginary quadratic fields of relative density $5/6$, we explicitly determine the $S_K$-unit solutions and verify the valuation bound for both criteria when $A,B,C\in\{\pm2^r:r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq0}\}$, including the same $r\geq5$ conclusion. △ Less

    Abstract: …, for the relaxed compressible Euler system with a Maxwell-type constitutive law for the stress tensor. Given a Navier-Stokes background satisfying suitable boundedness… ▽ More We study the global well-posedness of the Cauchy problem in $\mathbb R^d$, $d\geq2$, for the relaxed compressible Euler system with a Maxwell-type constitutive law for the stress tensor. We show that the corresponding compressible Navier-Stokes solutions serve as both relaxation limits and large-time asymptotic profiles of the global relaxed Euler solutions constructed here. Given a Navier-Stokes background satisfying suitable boundedness and decay assumptions, we construct global smooth relaxed Euler solutions for each fixed $α\in(0,1]$ and sufficiently small $\varepsilon>0$. Here, the background need not be small. After subtracting the stress initial layer, the approximation error is $O(\varepsilon^α)$ uniformly in time. Under suitable additional conditions on the initial perturbations, this error decays in $L^2$ faster than the decay of standard Navier-Stokes background, uniformly in $\varepsilon$, identifying the background solution as a large-time asymptotic profile. As an application, we obtain the corresponding small-data results using classical Navier-Stokes theory to verify the background assumptions. △ Less

In this paper, we propose Aligned Delta-Triplane Transformer (ADTT), a compact 4D OWM that explicitly aligns historical triplanes with ego-motion compensation and predicts only future scene changes. The predicted changes are added to the aligned prior and decoded into future occupancy scenes. Experiments on Occ3D-nus show that ADTT achieves state-of-the-art forecasting performance with fewer parameters and higher FPS. Our code is publicly available online: https://github.com/kyoran/ADTT. △ Less

    Local Convergence of Triadic Closures of Sparse Random Graphs

    Authors: Himasish Talukdar

    Abstract: …, then the corresponding sequence of triadic closures converges locally in probability to the same triadic closure of $H^*$. As a consequence, we obtain a general limit theorem for the clustering coefficients of triadic closures. In particular, we show that the triadic closures can have a strictly positive limiting clustering coefficients even when the limit… ▽ More Triadic closure is a natural mechanism for modeling the clustering phenomenon observed in many real-world networks. Given a possibly random undirected graph $H$, we consider pairs of non-adjacent vertices that have a common neighbor. Conditional on $H$, each such pair is joined by an edge independently and simultaneously, with a probability depending on the degrees of its endpoints and on the number of their common neighbors. The resulting graph is called a triadic closure of $H$. We study the effect of this operation on local convergence. More precisely, we prove that if a sequence of potentially random finite undirected graphs of $\left( H_n \right)_{n \in \mathbb{N}}$ converges locally in probability to a rooted graph $H^*$, then the corresponding sequence of triadic closures converges locally in probability to the same triadic closure of $H^*$. As a consequence, we obtain a general limit theorem for the clustering coefficients of triadic closures. In particular, we show that the triadic closures can have a strictly positive limiting clustering coefficients even when the limiting clustering coefficients of the parent graph sequence is zero. Finally, we apply our results to several classical sparse random graph models including Erdős-Rényi, configuration, preferential attachment models and compute the limiting clustering coefficients of their triadic closures. △ Less

Applying Follow-the-Regularized-Leader (FTRL) and Online Mirror Descent (OMD) on the… ▽ More A central topic in online and stochastic optimization is to design algorithms that do not require tuning of hyperparameters. Successful strategies in online learning include AdaGrad-style learning rates, Conic Blackwell algorithms, and parameter-free algorithms. However, these classes require different, often difficult, analyses. We develop a unifying family of Online Convex Optimization (OCO) algorithms that require no user-tuned learning rate on convex domains that may be unbounded. The construction lifts the domain to a cone by adding one scalar coordinate, an idea going back to Blackwell (1954). The lifted subgradients are orthogonal to the corresponding lifted decisions, leaving the comparator scale free to optimize in the regret analysis. Applying Follow-the-Regularized-Leader (FTRL) and Online Mirror Descent (OMD) on the cone recovers Conic Blackwell Algorithm (CBA) and Conic Blackwell Algorithm Plus (CBA$^+$) on bounded domains in Hilbert spaces and extends them to finite-dimensional normed spaces with a strongly convex squared norm. For arbitrary nonempty closed convex domains in these spaces, a known gradient bound yields parameter-free regret guarantees. Surprisingly, on an unconstrained Hilbert space, the construction recovers the Krichevsky--Trofimov (KT) coin-betting algorithm exactly. We also give a parameter-free algorithm for unconstrained domains that requires no gradient bound. △ Less

In the second stage, the rigidity map is processed using the same visual encoding design, while the pretrained skinning backbone… ▽ More Rigging is inherently task-dependent because the same mesh may require different skeletons and deformation behaviors across animation tasks. In practice, artists often inspect an initial rig and repeatedly edit its skeletal structure and deformation behavior to meet specific animation requirements. Existing automatic methods primarily generate a plausible rig from geometry, offering limited explicit control over the resulting skeleton and deformation behavior. In this work, we present ViP-Rig, a visual-prompted framework that supports both prompt-first rigging and result-guided editing by injecting features extracted from user-drawn or edited 2D skeletal and rigidity prompts into frozen pretrained backbones. Specifically, ViP-Rig consists of two stages, Skeleton Generation and Skinning Prediction. In the first stage, the skeletal sketch is processed by the Dense-to-Compact Visual Prompt Encoding to produce compact, fixed-length conditioning tokens. The resulting tokens are injected into a frozen pretrained autoregressive generator through gated adapters to control joint placement and branching structure while preserving the generator's geometric prior. In the second stage, the rigidity map is processed using the same visual encoding design, while the pretrained skinning backbone remains frozen. The resulting tokens are symmetrically injected into the point and joint streams to modulate point-joint compatibility and the resulting skinning weights. Experiments on Articulation-XL2.0 and zero-shot evaluation on ModelsResource show that ViP-Rig more accurately recovers target skeletons and skinning weights than geometry-conditioned baselines under prompt-guided evaluation. Qualitative results further demonstrate explicit and localized control in both prompt-first rigging and result-guided editing. △ Less

    Authors: Zhaoyu Chen, Hongnan Lin, Yongwei Nie, Fei Ma, Xuemiao Xu, Fei Yu, Chengjiang Long

    Abstract: Temporal Video Grounding (TVG) aims to localize video segments corresponding to a given textual query, which often describes human actions. However, we observe that current methods, usually optimizing for high temporal Intersection-over-Union (IoU), frequently struggle to accurately recognize or understand the underlying actions in both the video and query, thus reducing the effectiveness of these methods. To address this, we propose a novel TVG framework that integrates inversion-based TVG as auxiliary objectives to maintain the model's action understanding ability. We introduce three kinds of inversion TVG tasks derived from the original TVG annotations: (1) Verb Completion, predicting masked verbs (actions) in queries given video segments; (2) Action Recognition, identifying query-described actions; and (3) Video Description, generating descriptions containing query-relevant actions given video segments. These inversion tasks are entirely derived from the original TVG tasks and are probabilistically integrated with them within a reinforcement learning framework. By leveraging carefully designed reward functions, the model preserves its ability to understand actions, thereby improving the accuracy of temporal grounding. Experiments show our method outperforms state-of-the-art approaches, achieving a 7.1\% improvement in R1@0.7 on Charades-STA for a 3B model. △ Less

    Authors: Pengli Zhu, Ruxi Chang, Tianliang Yao, Yu Jiang, Shengyuan Liu, Yiyang Lin, Xiaoyi Duan, Shuang Wu, Yixuan Yuan, the Alzheimer's Disease Neuroimaging Initiative

    Abstract: …Specifically, BRIDGE integrates a conditional brain-to-gut latent transport module and a disease-evidence-guided cerebral pathway to capture subject-specific cross-organ correspondence and disease-relevant cerebral representations. For the cerebral pathway, a large language model (LLM) helps organize literature-derived descriptors into human-approved semant… ▽ More Alzheimer's disease (AD) diagnosis may benefit from gut metabolic information associated with brain-gut interactions, yet existing models largely analyze cerebral glucose metabolism alone. Although whole-body fluorodeoxyglucose positron emission tomography (FDG-PET) can provide paired brain-gut observations, routine brain examinations rarely include gut imaging, limiting direct use of this information for diagnosis. In this paper, we present BRIDGE (Brain and Gut Representation Integration via Distribution-Guided Enhancement), a two-stage framework for transferring brain-gut metabolic knowledge to brain-only diagnosis. Specifically, BRIDGE integrates a conditional brain-to-gut latent transport module and a disease-evidence-guided cerebral pathway to capture subject-specific cross-organ correspondence and disease-relevant cerebral representations. For the cerebral pathway, a large language model (LLM) helps organize literature-derived descriptors into human-approved semantic anchors that guide feature learning. Additionally, we introduce a counterfactual-guided residual fusion mechanism, which uses matched and non-matched virtual-gut evidence to supervise a confidence gate and dynamically regulate the contribution of transported cross-organ information. At inference, BRIDGE exploits complementary gut-related context using brain PET alone, without measured gut PET. Extensive experiments across public and private cohorts demonstrate that BRIDGE outperforms brain-only and cross-organ baselines, achieving AUC gains of 6.0% and 5.2% over its brain-only counterpart for CN/MCI and CN/AD diagnosis, respectively. Notably, its ability to transfer complementary brain-gut metabolic context while preserving brain PET as the sole inference input highlights its potential for clinically practical diagnosis. Our code is available at https://github.com/CUHK-AIM-Group/BRIDGE. △ Less

    Authors: Gauresh Raj Jassal, Jiaqi Li, Lei Feng, Nicholas G. Bravo-Frank, Hao Wang, Jiarong Hong

    Abstract: …(RIHVR) framework, we extract phase maps from HM images and then invert the reconstructed phase to generate fluorescence-like contrast, in which bright inverted-phase regions correspond to bacterial nucleoid content captured by FM signals. We show that this relationship holds across multiple bacterial species, growth stages, drug treatments, and post-treatme… ▽ More We introduce holographic microscopy (HM) as a compact, low-cost, label-free, non-specific alternative to fluorescence microscopy (FM) for quantifying bacterial physiological responses during drug screening. Using the regularized inverse holographic volumetric reconstruction (RIHVR) framework, we extract phase maps from HM images and then invert the reconstructed phase to generate fluorescence-like contrast, in which bright inverted-phase regions correspond to bacterial nucleoid content captured by FM signals. We show that this relationship holds across multiple bacterial species, growth stages, drug treatments, and post-treatment time points, demonstrating that HM can consistently capture nucleoid associated structural information without fluorescent labeling. Because HM phase images contain both whole-cell refractive-index information and internal nucleoid-associated contrast, they enable simultaneous quantification of cell boundaries, cell length, nucleoid size, and nucleoid occupancy. These measurements allow drug-induced changes in bacterial morphology and nucleoid organization to be monitored over time. Unlike FM, which can suffer from photobleaching and may perturb live cells through phototoxicity, HM relies on refractive-index variations and maintains stable signal quality for long-term live-cell imaging. By linking label-free phase imaging to nucleoid-scale physiological readouts, this work establishes a path toward automated, high-throughput, and experimentally simpler bacterial drug-screening assays. △ Less

    Authors: Olga Kharlampovich, Igor Pak, Alina Vdovina

The transformations preserve $2$-adic distance, which gives the binary covers and a… ▽ More Expander graphs have been central objects of study for over fifty years. Ramanujan graphs attain the asymptotically optimal nontrivial adjacency-eigenvalue threshold for infinite fixed-degree families. The original arithmetic constructions by Lubotzky-Phillips-Sarnak (1988) and Margulis (1988), and the iterative $2$-lift method of Bilu-Linial (2006) provide different approaches to Ramanujan graph constructions. Motivated by work on Mealy automata, we combine both approaches to give the following new arithmetic construction. For every prime $p\equiv 1\pmod {17}$, we construct a infinite tower of $(p+1)$-regular non-bipartite Ramanujan graphs on $2^n$ vertices, with label-preserving $2$-lifts between consecutive levels. These construction is strongly explicit: for fixed $p$, a neighbor or lift sign is computable in $O_p(n^3)$ bit operations. The construction uses fixed algebraic $2$-adic transformations, specified by finite data computable in preprocessing depending only on $p$. The main number-theoretic tool is Blasius's Ramanujan-Petersson theorem for Hilbert modular forms, applied through the Jacquet-Langlands correspondence to obtain the sharp bound $|λ|\leq2\sqrt p$ for every nontrivial adjacency eigenvalue of our $(p+1)$-regular graphs. The transformations preserve $2$-adic distance, which gives the binary covers and an explicit formula for their signings. △ Less

We propose CCFormer, a structured interaction architecture that separates cross-field retrieval from intra-history modeling. Existing methods normally struggle to reliably assign different reference motions to the correct target objects, thus a motion intended for one object may affect another. Meanwhile, some motion-specific tuning approaches are still facing another issue: they must run a new optimization whenever the reference video changes, making repeated transfers inefficient. To address these limitations, we propose RART, a framework that unifies object-wise motion extraction, target-region anchoring, and frame-aligned injection into a frozen video Diffusion Transformer for multi-object motion transfer. For each reference motion, RART extracts temporally ordered features from the masked reference video, and then conditions them on the target first-frame mask to form mask-anchored motion tokens. Frame-aligned motion cross-attention allows each generated frame to read only the motion tokens at the corresponding time slots, composing all object motions in one denoising process. During training, a motion-attention routing loss encourages each target region to attend to its corresponding motion tokens and reduces interference between objects. Therefore, RART can handle novel reference-target combinations without per-video optimization. Experiments show that RART achieves accurate motion preservation, efficient inference, and strong compositional generalization. △ Less

    Verifiable LLM-Guided Focal SMT Solving for Quantified Arrays

    Authors: Kunhang Lv, Rui Han, Fuqi Jia, Yuhang Dong, Feifei Ma, Jian Zhang

    Abstract: …five logics under 1200s timeout (including LLM latency) shows that LinguaArray solves up to 69.1% of instances and increases solved-instance counts by 105%--397% over the corresponding backends. ▽ More Large language models can often identify useful semantic structure in formal artifacts, but their outputs cannot be trusted as proofs or solver decisions. We study whether LLMs can instead guide formal reasoning systems through a narrow, solver-verifiable interface. We instantiate this idea in quantified array SMT solving, where modern instantiation-based techniques can fail when key ground terms and equalities are hidden by auxiliary variables, axiom guards, and nested array terms. We observe that many instances are largely driven by a small set of focal ground terms or constraints, similar to the backdoor sets in SAT solving. Building on this, we present LinguaArray, a framework where an LLM proposes a small Semantic Focus Set (ground terms/constraints), while a conventional SMT solver remains the sole proof engine and certifies all results. LinguaArray uses these LLM-suggested, solver-validated finite sets to significantly enhance solver capability, solving additional satisfiable and unsatisfiable instances that the backend alone fails to solve. It interacts within a finite time and then falls back to the backend solver to mitigate potential regressions. Evaluation on 1015 SMT-LIB instances across five logics under 1200s timeout (including LLM latency) shows that LinguaArray solves up to 69.1% of instances and increases solved-instance counts by 105%--397% over the corresponding backends. △ Less

Extensive experiments validate that SplitGS-Loc extends the utility of photometric GSFFs to accurate and efficient localization by exploiting Gaussian attributes, without per-scene training for feature fields or iterative pose refinement.     math.GR

    Complete regular dessins and non-metacyclic bicyclic 2-groups with cyclic commutator subgroup

    Authors: Wenwen Fan, Yuan Shi, Yun Tian Wu

    Abstract: …steps for the cyclic-commutator case. This settles Janko's isomorphism-classification problem in this case and yields a complete classification and enumeration of the corresponding regular dessins. ▽ More We study complete regular dessins with underlying graph ${\mathcal K}_{2^e,2^f}$, where $e\geq f\geq 2$, whose automorphism groups are non-metacyclic. Together with the cases of coprime orders and odd-prime-power orders, this is one of the basic classes needed for Jones's open classification problem for regular dessins of complete bipartite graphs. Within the 2-power case, the present paper treats the subcase in which the commutator subgroup is cyclic; it is the first of three papers devoted to the non-metacyclic case. Our approach is group-theoretic. The automorphism group $G$ of a regular dessin of ${\mathcal K}_{2^e,2^f}$ admits an exact bicyclic factorization $G=\langle b\rangle\langle w\rangle$ of type $(2^e,2^f)$ with $\langle b\rangle\cap\langle w\rangle=1$. For a fixed group $G$, two exact generating pairs give isomorphic dessins exactly when they lie in the same orbit under ${\rm A}ut(G)$. Thus the classification reduces to three group-theoretic tasks: determining the isomorphism classes of the possible groups $G$, characterizing their exact bicyclic generating pairs, and computing $|{\rm A}ut(G)|$. Berkovich and Janko divided the relevant non-metacyclic groups, through parameterized presentations, according to whether the commutator subgroup is cyclic, contains a cyclic subgroup of index $2$, or contains a cyclic subgroup of index $2^r$ with $r>1$. However, their classification does not identify which parameter values in these presentations define isomorphic groups; this problem was raised by Janko in {J}. We carry out these steps for the cyclic-commutator case. This settles Janko's isomorphism-classification problem in this case and yields a complete classification and enumeration of the corresponding regular dessins. △ Less

    PrinGS: Privacy Preservation in Gaussian Splatting

    Authors: Gaurav Behera, Abhinav Raundhal, Archisha Panda, Saumya Balina, Gargi Shroff, Ponnurangam Kumaraguru

    Abstract: …framework that directly anonymizes a trained splat. Given a trained splat and a user-provided reference view identifying sensitive content, our method localizes the corresponding regions in the underlying 3D representation and directly anonymizes them, ensuring that the sensitive information is absent from all subsequently rendered novel views while preservi… ▽ More 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has rapidly become the preferred representation for high-fidelity novel view synthesis and 3D scene reconstruction. However, the learned representation encodes sensitive visual information including human faces, license plates, documents, and computer screens, raising significant privacy concerns when trained splats are shared or deployed. In this work, we address the underexplored problem of privacy preservation in Gaussian Splatting by proposing a representation-level anonymization framework that directly anonymizes a trained splat. Given a trained splat and a user-provided reference view identifying sensitive content, our method localizes the corresponding regions in the underlying 3D representation and directly anonymizes them, ensuring that the sensitive information is absent from all subsequently rendered novel views while preserving the overall scene geometry and visual fidelity. We achieve this post-training anonymization in less than 1 second. Furthermore, we extend our framework with a privacy-by-design training strategy that prevents sensitive content from being embedded into the representation during optimization, enabling the generation of privacy-preserving splats directly from training images. Our method achieves a remarkably low Face Similarity Measure (FSM) of 9.79% at a retained Face Detection Rate (FDR) of 39.47% and makes sensitive text completely unreadable with an OCR Character Error Rate of 1.00. Our work aims to serve as the primary stepping stone towards privacy-aware 3D representations and motivates further research along these directions. △ Less

    eess.SY cs.RO math.PR

    Limit Theorems and Optimal Policies for Flock Flight through Poisson Forests

    Authors: Lav R. Varshney

    Abstract: …random forest exhibits an ultimate speed limit: above a critical speed, almost surely no infinite collision-free trajectory exists for a single bird. This paper formulates a corresponding bulk-transport problem for many birds. We construct an explicit directed corridor network in a Poisson disk forest and impose a common temporal headway at its waypoints. Th… ▽ More Inspired by birds flying through cluttered environments, Karaman and Frazzoli showed that high-speed flight through an ergodic random forest exhibits an ultimate speed limit: above a critical speed, almost surely no infinite collision-free trajectory exists for a single bird. This paper formulates a corresponding bulk-transport problem for many birds. We construct an explicit directed corridor network in a Poisson disk forest and impose a common temporal headway at its waypoints. The maximum current equals a minimum vertex-cut capacity and is achieved by repeated flight along vertex-disjoint paths. The same schedule maximizes completed traversals at every finite horizon, with constant forward speed and no internal waiting. For a forest belt of fixed depth, exact transverse superadditivity yields an almost-sure deterministic current-density limit. A uniform shape theorem for an asymmetric planar dual identifies the current when depth and width grow together. For diverging depth bounded by a constant times width, this current equals the width-first, depth-second limit and is independent of the positive aspect ratio. When diagonal corridors are available, bulk current is positive at sufficiently low obstacle intensity and zero at sufficiently high intensity. For stochastic demand, an upstream admission queue is stable at every rate below the finite-network capacity. △ Less

    Authors: Bo Yan, Siming Zhang, Siqi Li, Hang Yu

We characterize the tractability of recovering these latent correspondences, showing tractable pairwise assignment but generally intractable multi-client hyperedge matching. Achieving this goal requires composable quantum digital signatures (QDSs), which support integrity, authenticity, and nonrepudiation when integrated into quantum networks.     Abstract: …rubrics. This may be especially consequential for health information, given that Google's traditional algorithm was designed to prioritize quality sources, and inaccurate information carries health risks. We evaluated 3,611 benchmark health questions, comparing each AIO against that question's top five traditional results on source selection and quality. We also evaluated the AIOs' statement grounding in their cited sources and how accurate AIOs were against physician-written rubrics. Overviews appeared for 96.4% of questions but drew on substantially different evidence than traditional search: only 44.4% of the top five results were cited by the corresponding AIO, which instead overrepresented commercial health publishers (e.g., WebMD, Healthline) at the expense of academic and government sources (e.g., Cleveland Clinic, National Health Service). AIO-cited pages scored lower than traditional results on four of six well-established credibility criteria. The grounding of AIO statements to their sources was poor: after correcting for classifier error, an estimated 56.7% of cited statements were supported by their cited page, and a further 20.8% of all statements were factual claims with no citation at all. When AIOs were graded against physicians' rubrics, they earned 38.2% of all available points (e.g., accuracy 54.2%, completeness 21.6%), and among the subset of AIOs whose physician rubric anticipated a specific inaccurate or potentially harmful claim, 22.2% of AIOs contained the harmful content. Notably, the accuracy of AIOs was unrelated to the grounding of their statements. We recommend AIO literacy interventions for users and holding AIOs at least to Google's own health-ranking standards. △ Less

In particular, the method does not require solving any standalone Markov decision process throughout policy optimization, and is symmetric in the sense that it stays invariant regardless of whether we are solving the min-max or the max-min version of the game.     Satisficing Regret Minimization in Bandits with Knapsacks

    Authors: Qing Feng, Tianyi Ma, Ruihao Zhu

    Abstract: …still preserves the oracle's sub-linear expected standard regret bound if the satisficing threshold is unattainable. Finally, we establish the oracle conditions and corresponding satisficing regret bound for existing finite-armed BwK and dynamic pricing algorithms, and conduct numerical experiments on dynamic pricing with limited inventory to demonstrate… ▽ More We study satisficing regret minimization in bandits with knapsacks (BwK), where a learner sequentially selects randomized policies, i.e., distributions over arms, to obtain mean rewards above a satisficing threshold as frequently as possible while respecting finite resource budgets. The performance is measured by satisficing regret, the cumulative deficits of the selected policies' mean rewards below this threshold. Resource constraints introduce two difficulties: an algorithm can incur linear satisficing regret even with small standard regret as deficits to the threshold cannot be compensated, and random consumption can cause early resource depletion even when policies are feasible in expectation. To address these challenges, we propose SELECTwK, a general algorithmic template that uses a BwK learning oracle algorithm with sub-linear expected (standard) regret upper bound to search for and test candidate satisficing policies (i.e., policies whose mean rewards exceed satisficing threshold and whose expected resource consumption is feasible over the time horizon). Our regret analysis, supported by an LP-duality argument, establishes that SELECTwK attains constant expected satisficing regret when a satisficing policy exists. We further introduce a stronger regret condition that bounds the expected sum of positive reward deficits to the optimal one at each time step, under which SELECTwK achieves a sharper constant bound together with a sub-linear bound in the time horizon. As a complement, SELECTwK still preserves the oracle's sub-linear expected standard regret bound if the satisficing threshold is unattainable. Finally, we establish the oracle conditions and corresponding satisficing regret bound for existing finite-armed BwK and dynamic pricing algorithms, and conduct numerical experiments on dynamic pricing with limited inventory to demonstrate the empirical performance of SELECTwK. △ Less

Without assuming d… ▽ More Distributed zeroth-order optimization is increasingly applied in heterogeneous scenarios where agents possess distinct data distributions and objectives. This heterogeneity poses fundamental challenges for convergence analysis, as existing convergence analyses rely on relatively strong assumptions to ensure theoretical guarantees. Specifically, at least one of the following three assumptions is usually required: (i) data homogeneity across agents, (ii) $\mathcal{O}(pn)$ function evaluations per iteration with $p$ denoting the dimension and $n$ the number of agents, or (iii) the Polyak--Łojasiewicz (P--L) or strong convexity condition with a known corresponding constant. To overcome these limitations, we propose a Heterogeneous Distributed Zeroth-Order Compressed (HEDZOC) algorithm, which is based on a two-point zeroth-order gradient estimator and a general class of compressors. Without assuming data homogeneity, we develop the analysis covering three settings: general nonconvex functions, functions satisfying the P--L condition without knowing the P--L constant, and those with a known constant. To the best of our knowledge, the proposed HEDZOC algorithm is the first distributed zeroth-order method that establishes convergence without relying on the above three assumptions. Moreover, it achieves linear speedup convergence rate, which is comparable to state-of-the-art results attainable under data homogeneity and exact communication assumptions. Finally, experiments on heterogeneous adversarial example generation validate the theoretical results. △ Less

    On Proximity Gaps for One-Point Algebraic Geometric Codes

    Authors: Songsong Li, Chaoping Xing, Chen Yuan, Ruiqi Zhu

    Abstract: …and correlated agreement underpin the soundness of interactive oracle proofs. We establish these properties for general one-point algebraic geometry (AG) codes, extending corresponding results for Reed-Solomon codes. For codes of length $n$ over $\mathbb F_q$ with designed distance $n-m$, write $ρ=m/n$ and fix $ρ\in(0,1)$. For polynomial curves of degree at… ▽ More Proximity gaps and correlated agreement underpin the soundness of interactive oracle proofs. We establish these properties for general one-point algebraic geometry (AG) codes, extending corresponding results for Reed-Solomon codes. For codes of length $n$ over $\mathbb F_q$ with designed distance $n-m$, write $ρ=m/n$ and fix $ρ\in(0,1)$. For polynomial curves of degree at most $\ell$ and any $0<\varepsilon<1$, we prove correlated agreement with multiplicative slack $\varepsilon$ and soundness $O(\ell(\ell+1)/(\varepsilon q))$ whenever $e\le(1-\varepsilon)(n-m)/2$, with no additional genus penalty. This gives asymptotically good AG code families from Garcia--Stichtenoth tower of unbounded length over a fixed field with soundness bounds independent of $n$, beyond the RS restriction $n\le q$. We also establish mutual correlated agreement up to radius $(1-\sqrtρ-η)n$, with soundness $O(\ell n/(η^3q))$ for $0<η<1-\sqrtρ$. Our half designed distance proof combines auxiliary divisors, simultaneous Berlekamp-Welch interpolation, and error-incidence counting to obtain a common polynomial representation without a Polishchuk-Spielman divisibility lemma. Finally, two counterexamples on the Garcia-Stichtenoth tower show limitations beyond half the minimum distance, at radii below the Johnson bound and with positive slack. The first requires a positive constant soundness lower bound independent of $q$ and $n$, precluding a uniform $O(1/q)$ guarantee throughout the Johnson regime. The second fixes the field and lets the relative gap above half distance tend to zero while ruling out correlated agreement for every soundness parameter $σ<1$, with positive limiting rate and relative error radius. △ Less

    Gradient Hölder regularity for singular fractional $p$-Laplace equations

    Authors: Chao Zhang

    Abstract: …, together with convergence of the normalized energies to the corresponding quadratic energy. The regularity of the limiting equation yields an improved affine approximation. For bounded slopes, we prove that every globally Lipschitz weak solution of $(-Δ_p)^s u=0$ in $\mathbb R^n$ is affine and use this result in a compactness argument. The two estimates ar… ▽ More We prove interior $C^{1,α}$ regularity for globally bounded weak solutions to the fractional $p$-Laplace equation $(-Δ_p)^s u=0$ under the assumptions $n\ge2$, $1<p<2$, $0<s<1$, and $sp>p-1$, where $α=α(n,p,s)>0$. This resolves the open problem of gradient Hölder continuity for globally bounded solutions in the singular range $sp>p-1$. The proof is based on affine approximation and estimates independent of the normalized affine slope. When these slopes tend to infinity, we prove convergence to a solution of a linear nonlocal equation of order $sp-p+2$, together with convergence of the normalized energies to the corresponding quadratic energy. The regularity of the limiting equation yields an improved affine approximation. For bounded slopes, we prove that every globally Lipschitz weak solution of $(-Δ_p)^s u=0$ in $\mathbb R^n$ is affine and use this result in a compactness argument. The two estimates are combined in an iteration that preserves the required energy and tail bounds and gives Hölder continuity of the gradient. △ Less

    Bregman projections and partition design for accelerating finite Markov chains

    Authors: Michael C. H. Choi, Youjia Wang

    Abstract: …. The corresponding mixing time therefore cannot increase. To choose the partition, we identify the divergence of $G$ from stationarity as the exact worst-case divergence of $GPG$ over all $π$-stationary baselines $P$. This objective depends only on the block masses. We give an exact algorithm for concave block costs, including those arising from relative en… ▽ More We study Gibbs averaging of finite Markov chains with a prescribed stationary distribution $π$. Given a transition matrix $P$ and the Gibbs kernel $G$ induced by a state-space partition, we prove that $GPG$ is an information projection for every admissible Bregman generator $φ$, with divergences defined using transition probabilities relative to $π$. A Pythagorean identity characterizes this projection and recovers the relative entropy and weighted Frobenius cases. A dual characterization identifies $GPG$ as a kernel closest to stationarity among those with the same projection chain as $P$; for relative entropy, it maximizes the entropy rate. The identities also give monotonicity under partition coarsening. For every reversible ergodic baseline $P$, Gibbs averaging cannot increase the squared weighted Frobenius distance to stationarity at any positive integer time, and the inequality is strict unless $P=GPG$. The corresponding mixing time therefore cannot increase. To choose the partition, we identify the divergence of $G$ from stationarity as the exact worst-case divergence of $GPG$ over all $π$-stationary baselines $P$. This objective depends only on the block masses. We give an exact algorithm for concave block costs, including those arising from relative entropy and power generators $φ(u)=u^p$ with $1<p<2$, and a greedy approximation algorithm with a provable guarantee for $p>2$. For power generators $φ(u)=u^p$ with $p>1$, the objective is proportional to Tsallis entropy of order $2-p$: its minimization favors imbalanced block masses when $1<p<2$ and balanced block masses when $p>2$, while all $k$-block partitions have the same objective value when $p=2$. A numerical experiment compares the resulting projected chains with a local Metropolis sampler on a multi-modal $π$. △ Less

    cs.CV

    Rotation Equivariant Mamba for Vision Tasks

    Authors: Zhongchen Zhao, Qi Xie, Keyu Huang, Lei Zhang, Deyu Meng, Zongben Xu

    Abstract: …sensitive to image rotations, thereby constraining their robustness and cross-task generalization. To address this limitation, we incorporate rotation symmetry, an important geometric prior in many visual domains, into Mamba-based architectures. Specifically, we introduce EQ-VMamba, the first rotation equivariant visua… ▽ More Rotation equivariance is an important structural prior for a broad range of vision tasks, particularly in domains such as remote sensing and medical imaging where objects have no canonical orientation, yet it remains notably absent from current Mamba-based vision architectures. Despite the success of Mamba in natural language processing and its growing adoption in computer vision, existing visual Mamba models fail to account for rotational symmetry in their design. This omission renders them sensitive to image rotations, thereby constraining their robustness and cross-task generalization. To address this limitation, we incorporate rotation symmetry, an important geometric prior in many visual domains, into Mamba-based architectures. Specifically, we introduce EQ-VMamba, the first rotation equivariant visual Mamba architecture for vision tasks. The core components of EQ-VMamba include a carefully designed rotation equivariant cross-scan strategy and group Mamba blocks. Moreover, we provide a rigorous theoretical analysis of the intrinsic equivariance error, demonstrating that the proposed architecture enforces end-to-end rotation equivariance under the p4 rotation group. Extensive experiments across multiple benchmarks---including high-level image classification, mid-level semantic segmentation, and low-level image super-resolution---demonstrate that EQ-VMamba consistently improves rotation robustness and achieves superior or competitive performance compared to non-equivariant baselines on most benchmarks, while requiring 40-70% fewer parameters. These results indicate that embedding rotation equivariance not only effectively bolsters the robustness of visual Mamba models against rotation transformations, but also improves accuracy on data exhibiting rotational symmetry, with substantially improved parameter efficiency. Code is available at https://github.com/zhongchenzhao/EQ-VMamba. △ Less

    Fragmented Obedient Equilibria in Routing Games

    Authors: Zhengyang Wang, Achilleas Anastasopoulos

    Abstract: …obedient equilibrium (FOE) by combining user obedience with mediator optimality, and establish existence under a lower-hemicontinuity assumption on the obedient policy correspondences. Under affine edge latencies and independent mediator sampling, we derive an exact reformulation in first and second flow moments that preserves best responses and equilibrium… ▽ More We study routing games in which competing mediators recommend routes to fixed nonatomic populations. Each mediator chooses a randomized flow allocation to minimize its population's expected travel cost, subject to users having no conditional expected gain above a tolerance $ε$ from disregarding their recommendations. We define a fragmented obedient equilibrium (FOE) by combining user obedience with mediator optimality, and establish existence under a lower-hemicontinuity assumption on the obedient policy correspondences. Under affine edge latencies and independent mediator sampling, we derive an exact reformulation in first and second flow moments that preserves best responses and equilibrium profiles. Every feasible moment pair admits a finitely supported realizing policy. When the network has at most four routes, each mediator's best-response problem is an exact semidefinite program. A numerical illustration on the Braess network shows that relaxing obedience can reduce FOE costs, while gaps relative to the social optimum and optimal obedient pooling can persist. △ Less

    Nucleon Wave Functions from QCD Vacuum Topology

    Authors: Wei-Yang Liu, Ismail Zahed

    Abstract: …, close to experiment. The resulting covariant amplitude is analytically continued to the light front to obtain the leading three-quark wave function. The corresponding unpolarized, helicity, and transversity parton distributions, along with their QCD-evolved moments, provide a direct connection to lattice QCD calculations and global-fit analyses. Our result… ▽ More Instantons, as topologically nontrivial gauge field configurations, provide a microscopic mechanism for chiral symmetry breaking and the $U_A(1)$ anomaly in QCD. Within the instanton liquid model (ILM), instantons generate multi-fermion interactions governed by their density and size. Using this framework, we develop a microscopic description of the nucleon based on instanton-induced quark--quark correlations. We derive the diquark interaction kernels arising from single instantons and instanton--anti-instanton molecules, revealing a strong attraction in the scalar channel and weaker correlations in the spin-one channels. Embedding these interactions in a covariant, permutation-symmetric Bethe--Salpeter--Faddeev equation yields a nucleon mass $m_N=939.53~{\rm MeV}$, with a predominantly scalar-diquark component ($98\%$) and a small axial-vector admixture ($2\%$) around the mass pole. Despite its small weight, the small admixture generates substantial spin--isospin interference, yielding an axial charge $g_A=1.265$, close to experiment. The resulting covariant amplitude is analytically continued to the light front to obtain the leading three-quark wave function. The corresponding unpolarized, helicity, and transversity parton distributions, along with their QCD-evolved moments, provide a direct connection to lattice QCD calculations and global-fit analyses. Our results show that the topology of the QCD vacuum organizes not only the nucleon mass and spin structure, but also its leading valence partonic content, providing a microscopic foundation for a more complete description including confinement and higher Fock components. △ Less

    cond-mat.supr-con

    Local Josephson response near nonmagnetic impurities and vortices in a two-orbital $s_{\pm}$ superconductor

    Authors: Yuanyuan Zhao

    Abstract: …contributions of opposite sign nearly cancel in the clean sample. Nonmagnetic impurities redistribute these contributions and can reverse the local coupling without a corresponding $π$ phase shift of the bond pair fields. Negative coupling appears at off-center sites at weaker impurity potentials than at the impurity site. The impurity-induced change is conc… ▽ More We study the local Josephson response near nonmagnetic impurities and vortices in a self-consistent two-orbital $s_{\pm}$ superconductor. For a local $s$-wave tip that couples equally to the two orbitals, anomalous spectral contributions of opposite sign nearly cancel in the clean sample. Nonmagnetic impurities redistribute these contributions and can reverse the local coupling without a corresponding $π$ phase shift of the bond pair fields. Negative coupling appears at off-center sites at weaker impurity potentials than at the impurity site. The impurity-induced change is concentrated at lower quasiparticle energies, while states over a broader energy range contribute to the total clean and impurity couplings. In zero field, the coupling also changes sign as the tip gap is varied, and increasing the tunneling strength can reverse the $m=1$ sine component of the current without changing the sample state. At the chosen coherence-peak energy, increasing impurity strength mainly changes the relative intensities of existing quasiparticle-interference features. In a self-consistent two-vortex state, the Josephson response develops a spatially varying phase relative to the condensate. The response and condensate share the winding close to each core, while larger loops enclose additional winding of the Josephson response. The local Josephson sign and phase reflect the anomalous spectrum and the tip--sample coupling. △ Less

    Adaptive Bayesian Estimation of Conditional Densities Based on Orthogonal Polynomials

    Authors: Andriy Norets, Marco Stenborg Petterson

    Abstract: …. The posterior predictive density attains the corresponding minimax squared Hellinger risk. For bounded nonnegative representations in Legendre and Chebyshev classes, we establish the minimax rate at every $s>0$. A prior that also permits a small mixture with the reference density adapts over these classes up to a logarithmic factor and retains the exact… ▽ More We study Bayesian estimation of conditional densities represented by normalized squares of orthogonal polynomial series. We specify the conditional density without modeling the marginal distribution of covariates. A prior on smoothness, the number of terms, and the coefficients gives adaptive posterior contraction at the minimax rate without a logarithmic factor when the normalized square-root representation of the true density is bounded away from zero and sufficiently smooth. For one outcome and $d_x$ covariates, the rate over classes with degree smoothness $s$ is $n^{-s/(2s+d_x+1)}$. The posterior predictive density attains the corresponding minimax squared Hellinger risk. For bounded nonnegative representations in Legendre and Chebyshev classes, we establish the minimax rate at every $s>0$. A prior that also permits a small mixture with the reference density adapts over these classes up to a logarithmic factor and retains the exact rate on the positive, sufficiently smooth subclass. Additional results concern identification, conditional distribution functions and quantiles, coefficient recovery and uniform convergence, independent Gaussian coefficient priors, and selection of polynomial terms. △ Less

    ACAM-J-MARS: Multi-Atlas Riemannian Spectral Representations for Decoding Advanced Meditation States from fMRI

    Authors: Jian Wang, Winson F. Z. Yang, Wugeng Zheng, Matthew D. Sacchet

    Abstract: …performance across multiple backbone models, achieving a balanced accuracy of 0.757 for early versus late ACAM-J classification and 0.545 for three-class classification, corresponding to a 10.8-point absolute improvement over the strongest baseline. These findings suggest that the proposed representation improves the decoding of advanced meditative brain sta… ▽ More Inferring jhana-type advanced concentration absorption meditation (ACAM-J) states from fMRI data is challenging due to the spatially distributed nature of meditation-related effects, substantial inter-individual variability that can exceed within-subject state differences, and limited sample sizes. Conventional machine-learning approaches often formulate this problem as unimodal, Euclidean, atlas-based classification, thereby neglecting the non-Euclidean geometry of functional connectivity matrices and underrepresenting temporal information in functional magnetic resonance imaging (fMRI); these limitations can lead to suboptimal classification performance. We propose ACAM-J-MARS (\textbf{M}ulti-\textbf{A}tlas \textbf{R}iemannian \textbf{S}pectral representation), which computes functional connectivity using multiple atlases, represents symmetric positive definite (SPD) connectivity matrices on a Riemannian manifold, projects them into a log-Euclidean tangent space, and augments connectivity-derived features with low-frequency BOLD spectral energy to better resolve subtle state differences under heterogeneous variability. Evaluated on 204 segments from 22 practitioners, ACAM-J-MARS improves performance across multiple backbone models, achieving a balanced accuracy of 0.757 for early versus late ACAM-J classification and 0.545 for three-class classification, corresponding to a 10.8-point absolute improvement over the strongest baseline. These findings suggest that the proposed representation improves the decoding of advanced meditative brain states and may enable downstream applications such as closed-loop neurofeedback and real-time psychophysiological brain-computer interfaces. △ Less

    math.OA math.FA

    Locally unital $C^*$-algebras and limitations of seminorm criteria for compact operators

    Authors: Denis Fufaev

    Abstract: …-modules admitting a standard frame, all the corresponding notions of total boundedness coincide. ▽ More We study the problem of geometric characterization of Banach-compact and $\mathcal A$-compact operators on Hilbert $C^*$-modules in terms of uniform structures generated by seminorms. Previously, such a criterion for $\mathcal A$-compactness of adjointable operators was obtained by Troitsky for countably generated range modules with a suitable system of seminorms. Using locally unital $C^*$-algebras as modules over themselves, we establish fundamental limitations of such characterizations. Namely, we show that for nonunital locally unital $C^*$-algebras not only does Troitsky's criterion fail to extend, but, more generally, no system of seminorms can characterize $\mathcal A$-precompactness by total boundedness, nor Banach-compactness of bounded operators or $\mathcal A$-compactness of adjointable operators by total boundedness of the images of the unit ball. Moreover, the same statement holds for Hilbert $C^*$-modules $\mathcal N$ whenever the algebra $\mathbf K(\mathcal N)$ of $\mathcal A$-compact operators is locally unital and nonunital. We also study Troitsky-type systems of seminorms and their relation to $\mathcal A$-precompactness. We prove that every $\mathcal A$-precompact subset is totally bounded with respect to the strongest of such structures. Consequently, for Hilbert $C^*$-modules admitting a standard frame, all the corresponding notions of total boundedness coincide. △ Less

    cs.CV

    Joint Rigid Alignment and Morphable Reconstruction from Partial 3D Face Scans

    Authors: Eli A. Underwood, Nicolas Rondan, Angel V. Peterchev, J. Matias Di Martino

    Abstract: …brings the scan into a common frame, and completing the facial shape in regions the scan never observed. Existing pipelines typically decouple these steps, running a generic point-… ▽ More Aligning a partial, real-world 3D face scan to a canonical representation requires solving two coupled problems at once: estimating the rigid pose that brings the scan into a common frame, and completing the facial shape in regions the scan never observed. Existing pipelines typically decouple these steps, running a generic point-cloud registration front end followed by a separate non-rigid fitting or deformation stage. Building on the GeoTransformer superpoint-matching architecture, we propose a single network that predicts both a rigid pose and a shape-complete point cloud in one forward pass: a cross-attention regressor, conditioned on transformer point features and predicted coarse correspondences, maps a partial scan to per-patch coefficients of a 3D morphable face model learned offline from a corpus of 8,000 identities. The resulting morphed template is then used, in the same forward pass, as the target for fine-grained Sinkhorn matching and rigid pose estimation, supervised purely by geometric alignment to ground-truth morphed geometry, so no hand-labeled coefficients are required. Against six classical and learned non-rigid registration baselines on held-out identities, our method leads the strongest ones by 3-4 times on per-vertex error and baselines that collapse the head onto the visible region by more than an order of magnitude, and is simultaneously the most accurate and fastest method on ground-truth-free scans from two independent real-scan datasets, NoW and NPHM. Code will be made publicly available. △ Less
